## M23 — User feedback round 3: editing ergonomics, MIDI I/O & export depth

Seeded 2026-07-26 from the user's hands-on session against **`dist/DAWPro.app` built Jul 20 18:05** (user-confirmed) — that bundle carries m22-a…f, so every verdict below is against shipped code, and one report is a REGRESSION in the newest shipped item, not a standing gap. Every claim was verified in source before filing; the ALREADY-HAVE list is recorded so we never rebuild what exists. **Runs after M22 completes (m22-g P3 is still unlaunched) per the finish-M(n) rule.** Largest milestone seeded so far (17 items) — ordering deliberately front-loads the regression and the cheap high-visibility fixes ahead of the design-first subsystems.

**HAVE (verified, do not rebuild):** piano-roll transport playhead + scrub (`DAWAppKit/PianoRollPlayhead.swift`, m10-e — wired at `PianoRollView.swift:665`, fed by `store.transport.positionBeats` which the engine pushes via `playheadHandler` at `ProjectStore.swift:232` plus a 33 ms tempo-map fallback at `:5758`; **no paused TimelineView is involved, so the m22-e freeze class is ruled out**); black/white key DISTINCTION in both the sidebar (`KeyboardSidebar.isBlackKey`) and the grid stripes (`PianoRollView.swift:934`); dirty tracking + clean-quit autosave (`autosaveIfNeeded()` on `willTerminate`, `DAWProApp.swift:740`); EQ high-pass/low-pass with 12/24 dB-oct slopes (m22-a) — the "cut frequencies outside the instrument's range" move is already possible, what's missing is guidance; `saturator` effect kind (broadband — NOT a substitute for psychoacoustic bass harmonics); render surface `render.mixdown` / `render.bounce` (loudness normalization + `truePeakCeilingDb` = real mastering) / `render.stems` (+`includeMixdown`) / `render.measureLoudness` / `track.bounceInPlace` / `extract_stems`; timing alignment `take.autoAlign`; clip-level `pitchShiftSemitones`; own-voice conversion `vc.convertVocals` (RVC). **NOT built, confirmed by exhaustive grep:** ~~any Standard-MIDI-File read/write path~~ **[SUPERSEDED 2026-07-27 — SMF read AND write BOTH SHIPPED (m23-k1/k2/k3/k4a/k4b). This HAVE list is the 2026-07-26 SEED state and is now stale on this rung; any item pointing at it must re-measure first]**; any follow-playhead/auto-scroll; any note audition; any vertical track reorder; any multi-select of tracks or clips; any audio transcription (all `transcri*` hits are the copilot TRANSCRIPT, unrelated).

**Standing constraints every item inherits:** additive-only wire/MCP, never rename a live command; each NEW capability ships the CLAUDE.md triple — control command + MCP tool + test; own-voice-only policy is non-negotiable.

- [x] (m23-a) **Mixer strip layout: the m22-e insert-chip REGRESSION, plus panel collapse + a volume knob.** User report: "in Mix tab when it adds inserts and other instruments or effects it eclipses the volume scales." m22-e is what ADDED insert chips with five-cell activity bars, and the tested bundle contains it — treat as a shipped-quality regression and fix FIRST. Three parts: (1) the strip must reserve the fader/scale region so inserts can never overlap it (`Sources/DAWApp/Mixer/MixerStripView.swift`, 37.7 KB — the layout owner); (2) a collapsible instrument/insert top panel so manual fader work always has room; (3) a round VolumeKnob beside the existing `PanKnob` (`MixerControls.swift:99` is the precedent to mirror — same idiom, no new mechanism). Pure-view work: wire/MCP delta ZERO. Route: ui-design-engineer. GATE: staging captures at both densities with a full insert chain proving no overlap, Simple/Pro flip, suites. — **DONE 2026-07-26 (ui-design-engineer).** Root cause was one line: the fader's hard `.frame(minHeight: 150)`. A hard minimum is exported UP to a console that has no vertical scroll, so a growing chain made the whole mixer demand more height than the window had. **Blast radius was wider than the report:** at 760 pt the app header row AND transport bar were pushed clean off-window before any strip clipped; at 640 the strip clipped BOTH ends (an over-tall `VStack` centres and spills both ways — track name at the top, dB + Mute/Solo/Arm at the bottom). The master strip was a SEPARATE contributor with its own larger minimum; fixing channels alone would have left it. FIX: `MixerStripLayout` (new, headless `DAWAppKit`) splits a strip into a RESERVED cluster that never yields (header · VOL|PAN · fader+meter+dB · Mute/Solo/Arm) and exactly ONE yielding region (the Pro signal-flow block). `signalFlowRoom(available) = max(0, min(available*0.55, available − reserved))` — **content-independent, which is why it holds at any chain length**. The region is a bounded `ScrollView(.vertical).frame(maxHeight: room).fixedSize(vertical: true)`: `fixedSize` is load-bearing (a ScrollView's ideal height is its content's, so the region becomes inflexible at `min(content, room)` and the greedy fader absorbs the rest). Implementer REPLACED its own first cut — `ViewThatFits + maxHeight + layoutPriority(1)` passed the letter of its gate but left a 2-insert strip holding a ~200 pt void with the fader crammed at the bottom (a `maxHeight` frame stretches to whatever it is proposed): **the guard rail was reintroducing the failure it exists to remove.** Collapse disclosure PERSISTS as `panelLayout.mixerInsertsCollapsed` (console-wide, app-sticky, default EXPANDED so chains don't vanish on update; a disclosure, not a density mode → no chip; inventory verdict at simple-pro-inventory.md:216). `VolumeKnob` additive (`VerticalFader` untouched), Canvas contract honoured, new shared `KnobGeometry` is now the ONE knob-path home with `PanKnob` refactored onto it; cyan not neutral (volume HAS a meaning-color: fader fill, dB readout, VOLUME automation lane), unipolar from range-min with a unity detent so it cannot disagree with the fader about 0 dB. Explain 88→**89**. DELIBERATE, DISCLOSED DEVIATION — Simple mode DID change (knob row 38→52 pt + the knob; pan readout moved inline to `PAN L64`, a change driven purely by the Pro budget): **ORCH ACCEPTED** — the brief's "Simple must not change" was protecting against REGRESSION, and Simple's regression behaviour is verifiably unchanged (Pro sections still hide whole, freed space still flows to the fader); one row anatomy beats two forked ones, and the knob belongs in Simple most of all. ORCH VERIFICATION (own runs, own gate, final tree): Swift **3378/364 0-warn 78.4 s** + npm **207/207**; wire byte-identical to a launch-time SHA-256 baseline (`shasum -c` OK on BOTH `Commands.swift` and `server.ts` — note `server.ts`'s 1-line diff vs HEAD is the PRE-EXISTING m22-g Opus-5 string, so a naive `git diff` at close-out reads as a false wire violation). **The orch gate drove the chain to its real cap of 16 (`ProjectStore.swift:1441`) — the implementer's entire 44-capture matrix topped out at `full8`, i.e. HALF capacity, leaving the very scroll path its design introduces under-exercised.** At **n=16 × the 1208×640 WindowFloor**: track name, `INSERTS 16` badge, bounded scrolling region, VOL+PAN, fader, `0.0 dB`, and **Mute/Solo/Arm all present**, app header + transport on-window; Simple n=16 likewise whole; 10/10 captures distinct. ORCH FINDING, correctly attributed AWAY from this item: insert labels truncate (`Co…`, `Lim…`) on exactly the rows carrying m22-e GR mini-bars — identical in the HUG branch (no scrollbar) and identical in the PRE-FIX `before/` capture while `Saturator`/`Chorus`/`Delay`/`Reverb` render full, so it is **pre-existing m22-e, NOT an m23-a regression** — but it violates the label-never-truncates law codified last cycle, so FILED as m23-s. Residual the implementer flagged and the orch confirms: `maximalChainNaturalHeight = 366` pins the BUDGET, not the CONTENT — grow an insert row by a point and eight of them blow the ~5 pt margin with the test still green; the content-side model (`signalFlowNaturalHeight(insertCount:)`) is the fix if it ever bites. ORCH PART-3 VERIFICATION (the third of this item that only the implementer had vouched for): collapse driven over `debug.panelLayout` across a real process RELAUNCH (pid 10677 → 10739) — `mixerInsertsCollapsed:true` SURVIVED, so "app-sticky" is proven not asserted; collapsed strip shows chevron folded, **badge honestly reading the true count `7`** (not 0, not hidden), rows gone, SENDS/OUTPUT retained, fader long. Orch restored the shipped default (expanded) afterwards so no preference was left behind. TWO REFINEMENTS the orch captures force on the implementer's own framing: (i) the mid-row fold cut is NOT confined to "the window floor with a full chain" — at **n=9 on a 900 pt window** the OUTPUT picker already cuts mid-row (header visible, control a sliver); it is reachable by scrolling, so it is the disclosed residual and not a defect, but its scope is wider than stated; (ii) consequently the inline-pan-readout reclaim bought roughly **one insert** of headroom, not "a full effect chain fitting at the default window" — which does NOT disturb the deviation's acceptance, since that rested on Simple's regression behaviour being independently verified unchanged, never on the 13 pt claim.
- [x] (m23-b) **Piano-roll keys read as gray because they ARE gray — make them piano.** Verified root cause: `Theme.swift:15-16` sets `keyBlack = 0x11141C` and `keyWhite = 0x2C3242` — BOTH dark, so the "white" keys render dark slate. The user is right and the fix is small. Retone `keyWhite` to a readable ivory and re-balance the grid lane stripes (`PianoRollView.swift:934`) so the black/white alternation reads at a glance without breaking the dark-glass language or note-block contrast. Route: ui-design-engineer. GATE: capture at both densities, note blocks still legible over both lane tones, DESIGN-LANGUAGE compliance note, suites. — **DONE 2026-07-26 (ui-design-engineer).** The roadmap framed this as a retone; it is really an **INK-INVERSION** item, and the naive one-hex change ships a broken gutter. `keyBlack`/`keyWhite` have ONE call site (`KeyboardSidebar.swift:36`) but FOUR house-chrome elements composite on top of the white rows, all light-on-dark by construction — `hairline` separator, `gridEmphasis` octave marker, `textDim` label, `playback` cyan middle C — measuring **1.02/1.05/2.19/1.07:1** on ivory, i.e. invisible. The labels are not an edge case: they draw at `pitch % 12 == 0` and **C is always a white key**. Implementer found a FIFTH the orch brief missed: the gutter's full-height right-edge `hairline`, folded into the per-row dual-ink loop. FIX: `keyWhite` 0x2C3242→**0xD9D5CA** (the palette's first WARM token, so it reads as ivory not as a light-mode panel), plus three measured inks — `keyEdgeOnWhite` (black@0.24), `keyOctaveOnWhite` (black@0.38), `keyLabelOnWhite` (0x232833). LAW → DESIGN-LANGUAGE: **the gutter is the app's ONE light surface, and everything drawn on a key takes its polarity from THAT key**; a light surface always earns its own measured ink, never borrows the dark hierarchy. The stale `Theme.swift:26-29` contrast comment (ratios quoted against three DARK backgrounds only) was amended rather than inherited. Measured before→after: octave label **4.14→10.07:1** identical at all 11 octaves; key alternation **1.44→12.55:1**; ivory separator 1.20→**1.74:1**; octave marker 1.56→**2.55:1**. Middle C's cyan RETIRED for a bold label + inset left-edge anchor bar (`x:0,w:3,h:rowHeight−4`) per the arrange marker-lane idiom — a static landmark is navigation furniture, cyan is earned active-transport state (the playhead lives in this very view), and it measured 1.07:1 there anyway; bold-only shipped first and the implementer's own capture rejected it, so the bar exists because the pixels demanded it. GRID VERDICT: **deliberately UNCHANGED** (`drawBlackKeyRows` stays `black@0.22`) — lane ΔL\* 2.07 is above the ~1 JND and note-pill contrast is INVARIANT at 4.70–4.84:1 across the whole velocity/alpha range, so the roadmap's "re-balance" had nothing to buy; ceiling is ΔL\* 6.9 because the panel is near-black; `0.22→0.45` recorded as a one-constant option, not folded in. Gutter and grid cannot drift: both call the one `KeyboardSidebar.isBlackKey`. HONEST RESIDUAL the implementer disclosed rather than buried: the octave marker at 2.55:1 does NOT read as a free-standing rule — it sits at the C|C# seam so the black key above absorbs it and it reads as a shadow; moving it to the musically-correct B|C seam would desync it by one row from the grid's own octave separators. **IMPLEMENTER CORRECTED THE ORCH'S GATE DESIGN — the load-bearing catch of this cycle:** the orch gate drove `debug.panelLayout {rowHeight}` at 24/34/64 as the gutter's key variable, but `PianoRollView.swift:126` builds `PianoRollModel(notes:clipLengthBeats:)` with NO rowHeight, so the roll is permanently at `PianoRollModel.defaultRowHeight = 14`, while `panelLayout.rowHeight` drives the ARRANGE lanes (`ContentView.swift:582`) and `TrackListView`. The rails would have moved the arrange view, left the gutter untouched, and STILL produced distinct captures — so the orch's own distinctness check would have passed and the orch would have claimed rail coverage it never had. Orch re-verified the claim independently (one construction site, no `model.rowHeight` assignment anywhere, 128×14 = the 1792 pt content height the implementer measured) and rebuilt the gate around the geometry that actually exists. ORCH VERIFICATION (own runs, own gate): Swift **3378/364 0-warn 80.9 s** + npm **207/207**; wire byte-identical by `shasum -c` against the launch-time baseline. In-app captures at 1400×1000 and the 1208×640 floor, both densities, with notes seeded on both lane tones AND on both white|white seams (E|F 64|65, B|C 71|72) — the seams being exactly where a too-faint separator would show; at 3–5× zoom the ivory/black alternation, the C4 label in dark ink, BOTH white|white seams, the octave marker, the inset middle-C anchor bar, and cyan note blocks legible over BOTH lane tones all verify. The offscreen harness proves the gutter ALONE across the full C-1…C9 range (pitch extremes are unreachable in-app: 1792 pt of content, `.defaultScrollAnchor(.center)`, no scroll seam); the orch gate proves it IN SITU composited against the real grid, which is where the note-block regression risk lives — complementary, not redundant. ORCH PROCESS NOTE: a first suite run reported 3 issues whose identity the orch destroyed with its own `tail -6`; the clean full-log re-run is green with zero `✘`, and the first run was concurrent with the orch's own staging launch + gate, the exact load condition the two known flakes need. **Never pipe the suite through `tail` when the point is to catch failures.** Perf: the per-row right edge adds 128 `Path` allocations per draw (~33%), but `KeyboardSidebar` is not inside a `TimelineView` — it redraws on model change only, so this is per-interaction, not per-frame.
- [x] (m23-c1) **The note-editor playhead is invisible whenever the transport is outside the edited clip.** The user's "playhead doesn't move" report. **REPRODUCED by the orch pass 2026-07-26 before any code was written** (scratchpad `orch-m23c-repro.mjs`, staging 17695, captures cropped to the piano-roll region so the arrange playhead could not contaminate the comparison): **A** clip@0 with the transport stepped to beats 0/2/4/6 -> 4/4 frames distinct, the playhead moves; **B** clip@16 with the transport at beats 0/4/8 (outside it) -> all three frames BYTE-IDENTICAL `f79b3abec088`, nothing playhead-related renders at all; **C** the same clip@16 with the transport driven to 16/18/20/23 -> distinct again, it appears and moves. Clincher: C's beats 16/18/20 are pixel-identical to A's beats 0/2/4 (`ce146ee70d96` / `5f44768b8da6` / `d1e9cce98c96`), so rendering is provably and purely CLIP-LOCAL. The mechanism is live; the gap is exactly and only the off-clip case, as `PianoRollPlayhead.isVisible` specifies. **Blast radius is exactly two call sites, both `PianoRollView.swift` and both passing identical arguments: `gridPlayhead`:664 and `velocityPlayhead`:847. They must stay in agreement or the two bands disagree about where the transport is** (the Pro controller strip has no playhead at all, so DESIGN-LANGUAGE's "spans BOTH the note grid AND the velocity lane" is already exact and must stay that way). **This is a design-law amendment, not just a view change**: `PianoRollPlayhead`'s own doc comment and DESIGN-LANGUAGE.md:36 both codify honest absence — "never parked at an edge" — and the clamp-to-an-edge-indicator option contradicts it as literally worded. The implementer must state which law it is amending and ARGUE why a direction marker ("the transport is N bars that way") is not the lying-about-position the law forbids, rather than assuming the distinction. Route: ui-design-engineer. GATE: captures CROPPED to the editor region from the start (whole-window hashing has produced a false negative on this exact surface once already), covering transport before the clip, after the clip, and at BOTH edges — `isVisible` is inclusive at both, so the boundary is where an indicator could double-draw or flicker against the real line; grid and velocity lane agreeing in every state; suites. **DONE 2026-07-26.** Shipped as an *annotated* absence, not a filled one. **The law amendment, and why it is not the thing the law forbids:** the line's behavior is UNCHANGED — it still draws only inside `0…lengthBeats` and is still never parked at an edge; `DESIGN-LANGUAGE.md:36` was reworded to `honest absence: the LINE is never parked at an edge`, because the noun was doing load-bearing work the original left implicit. What is new off-clip is a VIEWPORT-space cue: a soft cyan edge wash under an SF Mono direction pill (`◀ 8 BARS` / `2 BEATS ▶`). The argument the implementer made and the orch accepted: what the law forbids is a false TIMELINE-space claim. The playhead's x IS its assertion about where the transport is, so a parked line is pixel-identical to a truthful line at that beat and the user cannot tell the lie from the truth — THAT indistinguishability is the harm, not edge-adjacency. The cue makes no positional claim: it is anchored to the scroll viewport so it does not move when the timeline scrolls (a property a parked playhead can never have), it is never a hairline, it never wears the glow recipe, its wash is a gradient capped at 0.28 opacity with no hard inward edge, and its content — direction + distance to the near edge — is exactly true. Two honesty details written INTO the law: distance is stated in the CLIP's meter (the v1 one-meter-per-clip-view policy) so bars are approximate across a meter change, and the label floors at 0.1 rather than ever rendering `0`, because a zero distance reads as "at the edge" — the parked claim itself. **Structure:** `offClipCue` is gated on the SAME `isVisible` predicate `lineX` is, so `lineX == nil` ⟺ `offClipCue != nil` — line and cue are complementary BY CONSTRUCTION, not by two call sites agreeing, and both bands derive their rect from the one `bandViewportWidth` that already serves all three bands. Headless in `DAWAppKit.PianoRollPlayhead` (+5 tests); new `DAWApp/Components/OffClipEdgeCue.swift` is Canvas-free so m16-a never applies. **Wire delta ZERO** (`Commands.swift` + `server.ts` shasum-verified against delegation-time baselines), the m10-c/sp-b pure-view precedent the law itself names. **Implementer gate 33/33** with crops dimension-asserted before any hash was trusted; both inclusive edges show a single narrow run (the line) and no pill. **Independent ORCH gate 8/8 — deliberately NOT a re-run**: theirs drove `transport.seek`, which forces a state invalidation on every step and so cannot distinguish a live surface from a frozen one (the m22-e class that already bit this project once, where every seek-driven gate passed anyway). The orch gate never seeks for its load-bearing check — it starts REAL PLAYBACK toward a clip at beat 16 and samples the off-clip interval: **5/5 frames distinct at beats 2.5→5.2→8.0→10.7→13.5, the exact interval that was byte-identical `f79b3abec088` in the pre-delegation repro.** That is the user's report, answered on the user's own scenario. Plus edge hand-off both directions, the two inclusive edges distinct from each other, and a regression guard that the in-clip line still moves (4/4). Visually confirmed: the unit switches BARS→BEATS on approach and the wash brightens with proximity, grid and velocity lane agreeing in every frame. Baselines advance: Swift **3383/364** 0-warn (sole failure = the documented `SoundBankHostingTests` T1 AU-prep flake, isolated 7/7 in 0.611s) + npm 207/207.
- [x] (m23-c2) **Opt-in follow-the-playhead auto-scroll.** Auto-scroll does NOT exist anywhere — verified 2026-07-26 that the only `autoScroll` symbols in `Sources/` are `CopilotRailUIModel.swift`'s rail chat. Add an opt-in "follow playhead" mode that keeps the playhead in frame during playback on the arrange AND the piano-roll grid, with the standard page/continuous behavior and a toggle that persists. **The two surfaces are ASYMMETRIC and this dominates the estimate:** the arrange already has a programmatic scroll seam — `DAWProApp.swift:328` `arrangeZoomScrollTarget` + `arrangeZoomScrollNonce`, consumed by the lanes' `ArrangeHScrollBridge` — so follow there is target+nonce on an existing mechanism. The piano roll has NO such seam: `PianoRollView.swift:490-494` says "the roll's plain SwiftUI scroller keeps its own offset — no programmatic anchor re-pin here (the arrange lanes needed an AppKit bridge for that)". **OPEN QUESTION to resolve FIRST, not an assumption to build on:** that comment is about zoom ANCHOR RE-PIN (hold a beat under the cursor mid-transaction), which is strictly harder than scroll-into-view — so plain `ScrollViewReader.scrollTo` may be sufficient for follow and no new AppKit bridge may be needed. Establish which, empirically, before committing to an approach. Keep the follow POLICY (page vs continuous, in-frame margin, the persisted toggle) shared across both surfaces — that shared policy is why this is one item and not one per surface. Route: ui-design-engineer. GATE: live capture of each view actually following through a multi-bar PLAYBACK (not seeks), the toggle off proving it does NOT scroll, persistence across a real relaunch, suites. **DONE 2026-07-26.** **The line's central premise was WRONG and was disproved before delegation, then again in code:** there is no `ArrangeHScrollBridge` and there never was — the arrange's programmatic scroll is plain SwiftUI (`ScrollViewReader` + a layout-real 1 pt marker), so the roll needed no AppKit bridge either and none was written. **Both false comments corrected as part of the change**: `PianoRollView.swift`'s pinch note ("the arrange lanes needed an AppKit bridge for that") and `DAWProApp.swift`'s `ArrangeHScrollBridge` name, which is where the roadmap line's assumption came from — the state renamed to `arrangeHScrollApplyTarget`/`Nonce`, and `DESIGN-LANGUAGE.md`'s piano-roll bullet ("an AppKit bridge would be needed") corrected too. **The scroll-mechanism question was decided by MEASUREMENT, as instructed, and the answer is the MARKER + PAGE.** Both policies were built and driven through the SAME 8 bars of real playback on the same session (6 tracks x 16 clips, 100 pt/beat, viewport 1090 over content 7200): page **4** scrolls / **4** offset reports / rtt p50 **15 ms** p95 21 ms over 119 samples; continuous **262** / **262** / p50 **49 ms** p95 61 ms over 100 samples. Continuous costs 65x the scroll traffic and **3.3x the main-actor latency** — 49 ms against a ~33 ms transport tick means the main actor is already behind the cadence it is following (the sampler getting 119 replies vs 100 in the same wall-clock window is the same fact from the other side). **The decisive column is `contentWidthChanges: 0` in BOTH**, which is what answers the delegation's stated worry directly: the marker WOULD re-lay-out scroll content per tick, except every target is clamped to `contentWidth - viewport`, which makes the lanes' `totalBeats` zoom padding (`availableWidth + max(0, hScrollApplyTarget)`) a no-op for follow. So the marker never grows content, no lane relayout happens in either mode, and the bar-gridline alternative buys nothing the clamp does not already buy while costing a per-surface id scheme the roll's clip-local coordinate space does not have. Continuous also has no honest macOS-14 implementation (`ScrollPosition(x:y:)` is 15+). The measurement scaffold (a `mode` enum + `continuousOffset`) was REMOVED after it answered its question; the table is baked into `FollowPlayhead`'s doc comment and DESIGN-LANGUAGE. **Manual-scroll policy: SUSPEND, and say so.** A scroll during playback stands follow down on THAT surface (per-surface; the enable flag is one shared slot) until the transport restarts or the chip is clicked, and the suspension is visible — the FOLLOW chip's third face, cyan outline with no fill and no glow, because a control claiming to follow while the view sits still is the dishonest-readout class this project keeps closing. While paused a click RESUMES rather than switching off. **Trap 1 (the roll's two axes) solved STRUCTURALLY**: the `ScrollViewReader` is scoped INSIDE the vertical scroller so `scrollTo` (whose `.leading` anchor carries y = 0.5) can never reach it — gate-proven, not argued: the keyboard-gutter crop is byte-identical `ed5bdc9c010a` across a whole following run. **Trap 2**: every tick hangs off `transport.positionBeats`, never a geometry callback. **Trap 3**: the m17-b double-confirm is KEPT but for a DIFFERENT stated reason than zoom's — not stale-geometry clamping (absent at fixed zoom) but durability against a later forced re-render, which is exactly what `debug.captureUI`'s `cacheDisplay` does. **Shared policy headless in `DAWAppKit/FollowPlayhead.swift`** (`FollowPlayhead` value math + `FollowPlayheadModel` per-surface runtime, 17 tests), persisted as `panelLayout.followPlayhead` (default OFF), new `debug.followPlayhead` echo. **Wire delta ZERO** — `Commands.swift` `873949d1...` and `server.ts` `85de1236...` shasum-identical to the delegation baselines. **THE GATE FOUND A REAL BUG THE UNIT TESTS COULD NOT SEE, on the user's own path:** `rearm()` cleared the offset baseline, so the FIRST drag after every play was unattributable — the detector could only record where the drag landed, and the next transport tick then paged the view back out from under the pointer. Fixed (`rearm` keeps the baseline) + pinned by `dragBeforeAnyFollowScrollSuspends`. A second gate finding was the advisor's catch, fixed before the gate ran: off-clip the roll issues nothing, so its manual-scroll detector had to be gated on the same predicate or a scroll follow was never contesting would flip the chip to PAUSED — proven by a controlled pair (same clip, same drag, transport outside -> not suspended; inside -> suspended). **GATE 43/43** (`scripts/gates/m23c2-follow-playhead.mjs`, staging 17695): every leg is REAL PLAYBACK, never seeks, with the playhead measured in PIXELS on crops of each band whose dimensions are asserted before any number is trusted. The fixture PINS every variable a crop window depends on (window frame, density, ppb and `rowHeight`) rather than inheriting it from `UserDefaults` — an inherited row height slides a window off the band it measures while the dimension assertion still passes, which is the m23-b "the variable never reached the surface" failure wearing the opposite mask. Following ON — 8 samples across 30 beats, the playhead in frame in every one on both surfaces, the arrange ruler and lane row agreeing on its x, the roll's velocity lane agreeing with the grid (the m23-c1 two-bands law), 3 arrange scrolls and 2 roll scrolls (page, not per-tick), 0 content-width changes. Following OFF — identical playback, both offsets pinned at 0, 0 scrolls, the playhead visibly GONE by sample 4 and consecutive frames byte-identical thereafter. Manual scroll — suspends the dragged surface only while the other keeps following, the suspended one does not fight back, and BOTH resume paths (chip and press-play) work. CTRL band — the third roll band was shipped-but-UNOBSERVED after the first gate run (it carries no playhead, and the fixture clip had no controller lane, so every capture of it was empty glass); the fixture now gives clip 1 a real CC lane whose alternating values draw one full-height riser per point, and under real playback the risers move with the page (sha 14fc1b018cd0 -> e23e6d20933a), stay on the note starts to within a pixel, and land where the REPORTED roll offset predicts (predicted 259/1059/1859/2659 vs measured 257.5/1057.5/1857.5/2657.5 at offset 1135) — "the three bands agree with each other" and "the three bands are where the follow model says they are" are different claims and both are now checked. Persistence — across a genuine relaunch, pid **24671 -> 24959** (23593 -> 23727 on the earlier 38-check run), the flag reading true where the default is false; the shipped default is restored before the gate exits, because this store writes through to the `UserDefaults` the user's own app reads. **Out of scope, stated:** roll ZOOM still does not re-pin an anchor (unchanged, and DESIGN-LANGUAGE now says why honestly); the Mix toolbar deliberately has no FOLLOW chip. Baselines advance: Swift **3402/365** 0-warn FULLY GREEN (~76s, the `SoundBankHostingTests` T1 flake did not fire) + npm 207/207. **ORCH INDEPENDENT VERIFICATION 2026-07-26 — the implementer ticked this box; the tick is earned, on the orch's own runs.** Reproduced: `swift build` ok, Swift **3402/365** 0-warn (the `SoundBankHostingTests` T1 AU-prep flake DID fire in the orch run where it did not in the implementer's — consistent with its documented load-sensitivity, and it isolates clean), npm **207/207**, and wire delta ZERO confirmed by `shasum -c` against baselines taken AT DELEGATION (`Commands.swift` 155 cases, `server.ts`). **The orch gate deliberately targeted the two claims the implementer's 43/43 does not reach**, both found by auditing their gate rather than re-running it: (1) **loop wrap — their gate never sets a loop** (`set_loop` occurrences: 0). `FollowPlayhead.landing`'s doc asserts "a loop wrap or a rewind pages BACK by the same rule" and `FollowPlayheadTests:41` unit-tests it, but that test exercises the PURE FUNCTION; it cannot show the LIVE observation→target→scrollTo path survives a wrap, where the position jumps backwards BETWEEN ticks instead of easing — the same computation-vs-wiring split m23-c1 codified. Driven with a real loop over beats 100–124 (1440 pt, wider than the 1090 viewport) at 140 bpm: the view paged forward to offset 6802, then on the wrap **dropped back to 5874** and repeated it on the second pass — backward paging is live, not just computed. Also confirmed `contentWidthChanges: 0`, corroborating the implementer's clamp argument that the marker never grows content. (2) **post-relaunch BEHAVIOUR — their L6 proves the FLAG survives, which is not the same claim as follow still working.** A flag restored without re-arming the mechanism passes L6 and fails in the user's hands (the m23-a persistence lesson, one level sharper). Fresh process **pid 25800 → 26397**, flag read back true WITHOUT being set, then real playback: offset 5872 → 6803, `scrolls=2`, never self-suspended. Visual confirmation on the orch's own capture: FOLLOW chip lit in the arrange toolbar, view scrolled to bars 26–29, playhead at screen-x **599 of the 1090 viewport** — in frame, which is the actual user-facing claim that a moving offset alone does not establish. Orch gate **13/13** across the two phases. **METHOD DISCLOSURE — two orch fixture bugs, both caught by the orch's own guard checks rather than by luck, and both the same class: a vacuous gate that a broken feature would also pass.** First, `debug.panelLayout` silently ignores `arrangePPB` (it is a `PanelLayoutStore` slot but NOT a `debug.panelLayout` key — the real seam is `debug.arrangeZoom {ppb}`), so the arrange content was 1152 pt against a 1090 viewport and follow correctly had almost nothing to do; the `0b arrange has room to scroll` precondition caught it. Second, the post-relaunch leg first played from beat 0, where 12 samples at the default tempo only reach x≈1008 inside a 1090 viewport — a correct follow does nothing there, so the check proved nothing until it was moved to start at beat 100. **Standing lesson: a follow/auto-scroll gate needs an explicit precondition that the surface CAN scroll and that the playhead WILL leave the frame; without both, the gate passes whether or not the feature works.** This is the same family as the m23-b lesson (a distinctness check proves only that the capture changed) and it cost two runs to see. **GATE-SCRIPT CORRECTION (same discipline the orch applied to the implementer's `tail`): leg 1c's boolean was sound but its PRINTED detail was not** — the first cut reset its `minAfterMax` tracker whenever a new high-water mark arrived, so it announced "offset returned to 6804 from 6804" while the real drop (6802 → 5874) was legible only in the trail. The 6802 → 5874 figures quoted above were read off the TRAIL, not off that check. `orch-m23c2-loop.mjs` now records the (highWater → lowAfter) pair AT the drop, before `maxOffset` moves on, so a re-run prints the number it actually measured. A gate that reports a value it did not measure is the same defect as reading failures out of a `tail`.
- [x] (m23-d) **Note audition: hear the pitch while you drag it.** Standard in every note editor and absent here (no audition/preview path in the piano roll — the `audition` hits in `AudioEngine.swift` are m22-g's reference lane, unrelated). Dragging a note vertically, and clicking a key in the sidebar, should sound the pitch on the track's instrument. Needs an engine seam to trigger a one-shot note outside the sequencer schedule, RT-safe and independent of transport state. Route: design pass by daw-architect on the trigger seam (must not perturb the render graph or the scheduled MIDI path), then audio-dsp-engineer. Triple required if a wire command is added (`note.audition` or similar) so the copilot can audition too. GATE: audible one-shot with transport stopped AND during playback without disturbing scheduled notes, no allocation on the render thread, suites. — **DONE 2026-07-26 (audio-dsp-engineer), built to `docs/research/design-note-audition.md`.** Five layers: a SECOND per-renderer `LiveEventRing` (`auditionRing`, cap 64, main actor = the single producer — made a type property by `@MainActor pushAudition`/`beatAuditionHeartbeat` rather than the doc's raw `.push` at the call site); `AuditionOutcome` + `setAuditionPitches`/`stopAllAudition` on `AudioEngineControlling` with protocol-extension defaults so the ~6 test fakes compile untouched; a `@MainActor AuditionController` in DAWCore (8-voice cap, 500 ms heartbeat, 30 s hold ceiling, last-call-wins one-shot); the wire triple `note.audition`; and the piano-roll drag + gutter press behind a debug-tier defeat switch (`debug.panelLayout {auditionEnabled}`, zero wire growth — the wire verb deliberately ignores it). **Liveness is a WATCHDOG, not a duration cap**: the renderer counts its own `renderedFrames` and cuts audition voices after 3 s without a heartbeat, so a crashed or wedged UI can never leave a note stuck, and no clock is ever read on the render thread. **DEVIATION FOUND AND AMENDED, not coded around (§6.3 → AMENDMENT A1):** the design's safety-release loop was unbounded, so on a dense-schedule quantum it wrote PAST the end of the preallocated merge buffer — reproduced first as `11 events out of an 8-slot buffer` (72 bytes past a 192-byte allocation) on the render thread, then closed with a computed `liveBlockBound` and a one-quantum DEFERRAL (6a does not clear the pitch map until the off is actually written, so the next quantum re-emits it — late, never lost). No capacity changed. Two smaller findings reported rather than built: §11.2's `soloExcluded` reason is unreachable given §7.1's merged `inaudibleMuted`, and §8.2's "reproduce the pitch↔y affine inline" was unnecessary (`PianoRollModel.pitch(forY:)` already exists; m16-a's `@Sendable` Canvas constraint binds the DRAWING closure, not a main-actor gesture handler). A hole the design did not cover was found at review and closed: `record()` now releases any HELD audition, because refusing new ones does nothing about a voice already sounding when the take starts — the heartbeat re-asserts it every 500 ms straight through the take and the capture ring cannot see it, so it would be heard and not recorded. **C18 staging gate 20/20** (`scripts/gates/m23d-note-audition.mjs`, port 17695, killed pidfile-exact): asserts AUDIO off `mixer.masterAnalysis`, never an ok response — transport STOPPED −80.00 dB floor → **−10.58 dB** sounding (+69.4) and back to the floor on release; DURING PLAYBACK the audition adds **+7.82 dB** over the running mix while the scheduled notes never fall below −14.44 dB, and after it releases the bus returns to within **0.98 dB** of the scheduled-only level (an `instrument.reset()`/`requestFlush()` would have collapsed it to −80 — that number IS the §5.3 proof); the debug switch OFF still sounds over the wire. Two gate-fixture defects were found and fixed by measurement, not by loosening a threshold: GM program 0 is a DECAYING patch, so both release legs were reading an envelope rather than the code (moved to the sustaining program 16), and "released" cannot be a single absolute dB — a note-off is followed by a release+reverb tail, so the gate samples twice 3 s apart and asserts the SHAPE (collapse −47.15 dB, then −57.73 → −80.00 = decaying tail, where a stuck voice would sit flat). §16 Q1 answered empirically: the first audition after `track.setInstrument` on a cold app answers `audible:false reason:"instrumentNotReady"` and becomes audible after **~250 ms** — `prepare()` from `setAuditionPitches` is necessary but the hosted instrument's async preparation is the real gate. C15 argued structurally line-by-line AND backstopped empirically: `malloc_zone_statistics` `blocks_in_use` delta **0** across 10 000 audition-carrying quanta, 4 runs (one-off probe, not left in the suite). 51 new tests / 5 new suites; Swift **3453/370** 0-warn (4 of 5 full runs fully green; the 5th hit only the known pre-existing `SoundBankHostingTests` T1 AU-preparation flake, which isolates 7/7 in 0.626 s), npm **212/212**; wire 152→**153** commands, 155→**156** router cases, MCP 155→**156** tools, Copilot catalog 65→**66**; both wire files' diffs are purely additive (`Commands.swift` +70/−0; `server.ts` +73/−1, the single deletion being the pre-existing uncommitted m22-g Opus-5 string). **REBUILD REQUIRED — `dist/DAWPro.app` predates this.** **ORCH VERIFICATION 2026-07-26 — TICK HELD (unticked by the orchestrator; the implementer had ticked it). Suites and wire are clean, but the FEATURE misbehaves audibly, so the box cannot stand.** Reproduced independently: Swift **3453/370** 0-warn fully green (the AU flake did not fire on the orch run), npm **212/212**, wire pins 153/156/66 with `note_audition` the only new MCP tool and the sole `server.ts` deletion the pre-existing m22-g Opus-5 string — additive-only holds. **C13 PASSED and is now measured rather than argued**: rendering the same project with a live audition sounding produced a BIT-IDENTICAL file (`eb006812a9338a36…`, 1156096 bytes both), so a held audition can never reach an export. **The defect: the audition note is released immediately instead of held, and a SECOND note sounds at t≈`durationMs`.** The control that makes it airtight is internal to one session on the built-in polySynth — a SCHEDULED 6-beat note holds FLAT at −14.5 dB for 6 s, while a `durationMs:4000` audition traces −12.9 → −77.7 over 4.1 s, which is the exact shape of that instrument's release envelope (measured separately with a 0.25 s scheduled note: −11.0 → −80 over ~4.5 s). Rises >8 dB occur ONLY at 0.15 s and 4.26 s, and the second tracks `durationMs` exactly (2.27 s for 2000, 4.26 s for 4000). **That >8 dB detector alone could NOT have exonerated the heartbeat** — a re-push of an already-open pitch lands on a voice already in release, so it would re-onset QUIETER than the previous peak and never trip an 8 dB rise; sampling at 150 ms cannot resolve a 500 ms sawtooth either. So it was measured instead: a dedicated 50 ms probe (`probe-sawtooth.mjs`, 58 samples over a 3 s audition, threshold dropped to ANY +0.4 dB deviation) found the decay **strictly monotonic** — the only upward step in the whole trail is the onset itself at 0.11 s. No sub-threshold re-onsets, no 500 ms sawtooth: the heartbeat is genuinely idempotent and renderer identity is stable, and the fault is at the INITIAL push, not in the re-assert. User-facing, this is the inverse of the item's own sentence: dragging a note gives a decaying blip and letting go gives a second blip. **Why the implementer's 20/20 gate passed: its release leg asserts the bus collapses and is still falling — which a note that NEVER HELD also does, from t=0. Nothing asserted the level was FLAT during the hold.** C8/C9 are sound unit tests that cannot reach this, since they bump the heartbeat by hand and so prove the render side HONORS a heartbeat, not that the live path sustains a voice. **ORCH METHOD DISCLOSURE:** my own first discriminator was an absolute dB threshold and it failed at −24.89 on a trail that was collapsed-and-still-falling; the implementer had already documented that "released" cannot be a single absolute dB and I wrote it anyway. I replaced the discriminator with SHAPE rather than loosening the number, and added a NEGATIVE CONTROL at the same sampling offset with no wedge — **and the control is what found the bug**, by expecting a still-held note and measuring −76.7 dB. Without it I would have reported the watchdog as proven. **Consequence: the main-actor-wedge watchdog claim is NOT yet proven** (the wedge seam itself works — the port refuses non-watchdog commands while wedged, and the app survives) because no valid discriminator exists while every audition decays from t=0; re-run that leg after the fix. **The re-verification gate was then CALIBRATED AGAINST THE LIVE DEFECT** (`orch-m23d-sustain.mjs`), which is only possible while the bug still exists: on the broken build the two new assertions FAIL for exactly the right reasons (flat-during-hold spread **35.3 dB** against a 6 dB limit; onset count **2**, the second at 2.09 s tracking `durationMs:2000`) while three validity legs PASS — the same flat check REJECTS a known release envelope, the same counter SEES a two-note stimulus as 2, and, load-bearing, a SCHEDULED long note on this instrument is flat to **spread 0.0 dB**. That last leg is what makes S1 an honest bar rather than an impossible one: the check is shown able to reject a bad shape AND able to accept a good one, closing both vacuity directions. After the fix all 7 legs must be green. Full findings + scripts in the session scratchpad (`m23d-orch-findings.md`, `orch-m23d.mjs`, `orch-m23d-sustain.mjs`, `probe-sawtooth.mjs`, `probe-decay.mjs`, `probe-release.mjs`). **ROOT CAUSE + FIX 2026-07-26 (audio-dsp-engineer), ORCH RE-VERIFIED — TICK NOW EARNED AND RESTORED.** One line explained both symptoms: step 7's silence fast-path (`schedule == nil, liveCount == 0`) returns BEFORE step 9's `instrument.render(...)`, so an instrument holding an open voice is skipped on every event-free quantum. The note therefore sounded for exactly ONE quantum, and the note-OFF — being an event — made `liveCount == 1`, skipping the fast path and rendering the still-open voice at full level once more: **the note spoke when it was told to stop.** That also explains my own measurement: the smooth monotonic decay I recorded was `mixer.masterAnalysis` PEAK-HOLD BALLISTICS, not the synth — I was watching a meter fall, not a voice release, which is why the 50 ms sawtooth probe found it perfectly monotonic. And it explains why LEG B (during playback) looked perfect (`schedule != nil`, so the fast path never fires) and why all 51 event-level tests passed: they use `EventCaptureInstrument`, which writes no audio and **structurally cannot observe a skipped render**. The file's own comment on that line claimed "live thru must keep sounding" — an intent the code did not deliver. Fix: a `liveTailRemaining` frame countdown armed after both drains (so the quantum that OPENS a voice arms it), aged otherwise, zeroed on both `instrument.reset()` paths, with step 7 gaining `liveTailRemaining == 0`; a countdown rather than a deadline on `renderedFrames` is wrap-free by construction, and zero-init means a never-sounded node takes the fast path from its first quantum. Cost is one `UInt64` and one comparison — RT-safe, no allocation. `liveTailSeconds = 8.0` is DERIVED, not chosen: both built-in instruments clamp release to `releaseRange.upperBound == 8` and both ramps reach exactly zero there. Because the arm condition is `liveCount > 0 || openAuditionCount > 0`, a HELD note re-arms every quantum and sustains indefinitely — the 8 s is the post-release tail, not a hold cap. **ORCH INDEPENDENT VERIFICATION (my runs, not theirs):** Swift **3456/371** (+3 tests) with the ONLY failure the documented `SoundBankHostingTests` T1 AU-preparation flake, which isolated **7/7 in 0.660 s** — same load-sensitive signature memory says never to chase, in AU sound-bank hosting and untouched by this fix; npm **212/212**; wire UNCHANGED and proven so by mtime (neither `Commands.swift` nor `server.ts` was modified during the fix pass) plus `allCommands` **153** and an additive-only diff (`Commands.swift` +70/−0; `server.ts` +72/−1, the sole deletion re-confirmed as the pre-existing m22-g Opus-5 string — +72 is MEASURED here; this record's earlier +73 was the implementer's own count, so the one-line gap is a counting difference, not drift in a file that should not have drifted). **My 7-leg sustain gate: 7/7 GREEN on a fresh app** — S1 flat spread **0.0 dB** (was 35.3 on the broken build), S2 **exactly 1 onset** (was 2) — and, load-bearing, **both validity legs STILL DISCRIMINATE after the fix** (S1-neg still rejects a release envelope at 33.7 dB spread, S2-neg still counts a two-note stimulus as 2), so the checks did not quietly become vacuous on turning green. **`orch-m23d.mjs` 12/12, and the watchdog claim is now PROVEN rather than unfalsified**: leg 1f's wedged note collapses (−28.8 → −30.5 → −32.6 → −34.7, vs sounding −4.73) while leg 1h's no-wedge control AT THE SAME OFFSET is still held and flat (−7.2 → −9.4 → −7.3 → −9.4, collapsed false, falling false). Before the fix 1h read −76.7 → −78.2, which is exactly why 1f proved nothing — everything decayed regardless — so this is the first run in which that leg could discriminate at all. C13 re-confirmed under the fix (same sha `eb006812a9338a36`, 1156096 bytes). The new `AuditionSustainTests` correctly uses a REAL `PolySynthInstrument`, pre-dirties the buffer with `999` sentinels, and asserts AUDIO (`peak > 0.01`, flatness `hi/lo < 2.0`) — closing the structural blind spot that let this ship. The implementer also ran the negative control properly, reverting the one fix line and re-measuring (flat check fails at 25.75 dB; 2 onsets, the second exactly at `durationMs`) — and disclosed that the old release check still PASSED there, confirming it never discriminated. **DISCLOSED LIMITATION, filed not buried: the identical hole remains OPEN on the live-thru path** (thru has no `openAuditionCount` equivalent, so the tail expires under a note still physically held); the tail mitigates it from one quantum to 8 s without closing it. Filed as **m23-u** with fix shape and routing, and placed in the `Order:` line. **REBUILD REQUIRED — `dist/DAWPro.app` predates all of this.** — **FIX LANDED 2026-07-26 (audio-dsp-engineer); box RE-TICKED — the orchestrator's own stated bar was "after the fix all 7 legs must be green" and they are, alongside 12/12 on the wedge legs and a negative control on the fix itself; orch re-verification is still expected, but an unticked box carrying a "fix landed" record is the one state `/dev-cycle` would re-pick and re-implement.** **ROOT CAUSE — one line, both symptoms:** `InstrumentSourceNode.swift` step 7's silence fast-path (`if schedule == nil, liveCount == 0`) returns BEFORE step 9's `instrument.render(...)`, so an instrument holding an OPEN voice was never asked to render on any quantum that carried no events. The note therefore sounded for exactly ONE quantum (the smooth 4.5 s decay the orch measured was `mixer.masterAnalysis` peak-hold BALLISTICS, not the synth), and the note-OFF — being an event — made `liveCount == 1`, skipping the fast path and rendering the still-open voice at full level for one more quantum, which is the "second note at t≈`durationMs`". It also explains why the orch's LEG B looked perfect (during playback `schedule != nil`, so the fast path never fires) and why all 51 event-level tests passed: they use `EventCaptureInstrument`, which records events and writes NO audio, so it structurally cannot observe a skipped render. The file's own comment at the `schedule == nil` load claimed "live thru must keep sounding" — an intent the code did not deliver. **FIX:** a `liveTailRemaining` frame COUNTDOWN (armed to `liveTailFrames` after BOTH drains whenever `liveCount > 0 || openAuditionCount > 0`, so the quantum that OPENS a voice arms it; aged by `frameCount` otherwise; zeroed on the two `instrument.reset()` paths where silence is contracted), and step 7 gains `liveTailRemaining == 0`. A countdown rather than a deadline on `renderedFrames` deliberately: wrap-free by construction, and zero-initialised means a node that never sounded takes the fast path from its first quantum. `liveTailSeconds = 8.0` is DERIVED, not chosen — both built-in instruments clamp release to `releaseRange.upperBound == 8` and their ramp reaches exactly zero there, so the tail cannot chop a built-in voice; a hosted AU ringing longer than 8 s past its last note-off is truncated, documented rather than left unbounded. No allocation, no lock, no clock: one `UInt64` and one comparison. **REPRODUCTION FIRST, offline and deterministic:** new `Tests/DAWEngineTests/AuditionSustainTests.swift` drives a REAL `PolySynthInstrument` and asserts on AUDIO (the harness dirties the buffer with `999` sentinels before every pull, so a path that neither writes nor zero-fills cannot read as silence by accident) — it failed exactly as predicted (`(lo → 0.0) > 0.01`; `(onsets → [93]).isEmpty` where quantum 93 IS the release quantum) and is green after. **TWO EXISTING ASSERTIONS WERE THEMSELVES THE BUG** and were corrected with comments rather than deleted: `LiveThruRenderTests`'s "ring drained → silent again" fired with both notes still HELD, and `AuditionRenderTests` C4's "nothing open → honestly silent" cut the voice's own release dead; both now assert the node keeps rendering AND that the tail is BOUNDED (silence returns after `liveTailSeconds`). **GATE STRENGTHENED with the two assertions whose absence hid this** (`scripts/gates/m23d-note-audition.mjs`, now 23 checks): a time-stamped `sampleSeries` across the hold AND past the release feeds (1) **FLAT across the middle of the hold** — measured spread **2.64 dB** over 14 samples in [750,2250] ms against a 6 dB tolerance — and (2) **EXACTLY ONE onset**, with adjacent rises inside 300 ms coalesced so an attack sampled mid-rise cannot read as two. The old release check's `-40 dB` threshold FAILED after the fix at −23.51 dB and was re-derived rather than loosened: it is now `3 x` the flatness tolerance (a stuck note is by definition still inside the sustain band), because **the 40 dB number was itself an artifact of the bug** — a note that sounded for one quantum had been decaying for four seconds by the time that check sampled. **NEGATIVE CONTROL RUN (the orch's own method, which is what found this):** the single fix line was reverted, rebuilt, and the strengthened gate re-run — flat check FAILS at **spread 25.75 dB**, onset check FAILS with **2 onsets at t = 104 ms and 3069 ms** (the second landing exactly at `durationMs`), while the OLD release check still PASSED, confirming it never had discriminating power; the line was then restored and the binary re-verified. **THE ORCH'S OWN 7-LEG BAR IS MET:** `orch-m23d-sustain.mjs` **7/7** on the built-in polySynth (a different instrument path from this gate's GM sound bank) — S1 flat **spread 0.0 dB / drift −0.0 dB** (was 35.3 dB broken), S2 **1 onset** at 0.16 s over 4.5 s (was 2), with both discriminator-validity legs still rejecting a release envelope and still seeing a two-note stimulus as 2. **THE MAIN-ACTOR-WEDGE WATCHDOG CLAIM IS NOW PROVEN, not merely unfalsified:** `orch-m23d.mjs` **12/12** — leg 1f cuts the voice under a real 3.6 s wedge (−28.8 → −34.7, collapsed and falling) and, load-bearing, leg 1h's NO-WEDGE control at the same offset now shows the note genuinely **HELD and flat** (−8.9 → −7.3 → −9.0 → −9.0, "collapsed false, falling false"). That control could not have passed before the fix, which is precisely why the claim was unprovable then. C13 re-confirmed UNDER the fix: both renders still 1156096 bytes, sha `eb006812a9338a36…` — the tail changes no export byte. **RESIDUAL DEFECT FILED, NOT FIXED (needs an architect call):** the THRU path has the identical hole. The tail mitigates it (a held thru note now sounds for `liveTailSeconds` instead of one quantum) but does not close it, because thru tracks no open-voice count the way audition tracks `openAuditionCount`, so the tail expires under a still-held note. It is masked in practice — `schedule == nil` only holds on a node where a schedule was NEVER published, and after any playback one stays published — which is why m3-vii shipped without a report. Closing it means adding an `openLiveCount` maintained across 6b's ID-pairing branches, which the m16-b3 §4.3 C11 rules govern and which nothing in m23-d's gate covers; the `LiveThruRenderTests` assertion above now documents it in place. Suites after the fix: Swift **3456/371** (+3 tests/+1 suite, the new sustain suite) 0-warn, fully green on a clean run; a loaded run hit only the documented pre-existing `SoundBankHostingTests` T1 AU-PREPARATION flake (main-actor setup, before any rendering — structurally unreachable from this change), which isolates **7/7 in 0.634 s** at load 7.55. npm **212/212**. Wire pins UNCHANGED and re-verified — `allCommands` **153**, router cases **156**, MCP tools **156** (`note_audition` present), Copilot catalog **66**: the fix touches `Sources/DAWEngine` + tests + the gate script only, zero wire surface. Staging 17695 throughout, killed pidfile-exact, port confirmed free and 17600 never touched. **REBUILD REQUIRED — `dist/DAWPro.app` still predates all of this.**

Fixed with the `captureUI:4772` idiom: `awaitPointerStageApplied(after:)` spins the main runloop, BOUNDED, until the lanes report back (new `arrangePointerReportSeq`, bumped by the view's own `onPointerState`) or a 0.30 s deadline passes — so it stops the instant the view has run and can never hold the main actor if no lanes view exists to report. The gate now reads the CAUSING call throughout, which is the stronger assertion (a follow-up read cannot tell "this call did it" from "an earlier one did"). **SECOND ORCH FINDING — REFUTED, with a measurement.** `pointerZone` and the create path do NOT use different y conventions: both consume the SAME `pointerLanes` array (`TimelineLanesView.swift:1125` and `:1221`), bands `[laneTop(i), laneTop(i)+rowHeight)`. Fixed by clamping both store import paths to `ClipEdit.minClipLengthBeats` (1/32 beat) — the PROJECT-WIDE trim floor already shared by trim/stretch/take-lanes/loop-ruler, so a degenerate import now lands on exactly the minimum a user can reach by trimming, not on an invented number. **HONEST LIMIT ON THE REPRO:** the implementer did NOT identify which callback macOS withheld in the user's session. Two evidence classes were kept separate rather than conflated — OBSERVED with zero injection (a hover armed by a drag that never terminates persists indefinitely, and was measured surviving app deactivation at 224 px), versus INJECTED (a post-drop `dropUpdated`, an ordering AppKit does not actually send after `performDragOperation:`). The fix is therefore deliberately TRIGGER-AGNOSTIC, in three independent layers: (1) `ArrangeDropSession` refuses a post-drop `update` and treats an `update` in `.idle` as an implicit enter; (2) **the user's next ordinary mouse move over the timeline clears any standing hover** (`pointerMayDismissHover`, guarded on `NSEvent.pressedMouseButtons == 0` so it can never cancel a live drag) — this is the layer that makes the artifact removable regardless of WHICH callback was withheld, which is what lets the fix be honest about not knowing; (3) the zero-length clamp. **ONE HOME, ENFORCED BY THE TYPE SYSTEM:** new `Sources/DAWAppKit/ArrangeDropSnap.swift` is the ONE place a landing beat is computed (the `MixerStripLayout`/`KnobGeometry`/`PianoRollPlayhead`/`EQFilterResponse` tradition), and divergence is made UNREPRESENTABLE rather than merely absent: `ResolvedDropBeat.init` is `fileprivate`, so `ArrangeDropSnap.resolve` is the only thing in the program that can produce one, `AudioImportContext.startBeat` is typed as `ResolvedDropBeat`, and `AudioImportContext` no longer carries `snap`/`meterMap` at all — there is nothing left downstream to re-derive. `AudioImportPlan` now does `context.startBeat.beat` and no snapping. **MAGNET PRECEDENCE, NOT NEAREST-WINS — the implementer corrected the orchestrator's brief here and was right:** beat 0 is a grid line for EVERY `ClipSnap` division and `ClipSnap.snap` rounds to the nearest line, so `distance(grid, raw) <= distance(0, raw)` ALWAYS holds; a bar-1 magnet competing with the grid as a peer can tie but never win, and clip-edge magnets die whenever the grid is fine. So the brief's own wording ('when the magnet target is nearer') was provably vacuous. Implemented as precedence inside a bounded radius, nearest-among-magnets, ties to the lower beat. **Threshold 10 pt, derived against existing numbers rather than invented:** strictly larger than `ArrangePointer.playheadGrabTolerance` (4 pt, a precision hit-test — a magnet is a coarse placement assist), and small enough that at `ArrangeZoom.defaultPixelsPerBeat` (16) it spans 0.625 beat so it can never pull across a whole beat at default zoom, nor across a 4/4 bar at any legal zoom down to the 4 pt/beat floor (10/4 = 2.5 < 4). The zoom-out consequence is documented in the source rather than left to be discovered. `ClipSnap.off` does NOT magnetise — the user turned snapping off on purpose. **STAGING SEAM `debug.arrangeDrop`** drives the same `AudioLaneDropCore` the real delegate drives (a real Finder drag is undriveable for two independent reasons: `DropInfo` has no public initializer, and the unbundled staging binary has no Accessibility grant). All 6 pristine files restored and SHA-verified byte-identical afterward. **ORCH INDEPENDENT VERIFICATION (my runs, my own script — 29/29):** `swift build` clean 0 warnings (the SourceKit diagnostics reported against `ResolvedDropBeat`/`ArrangeDropSession`/`AuditionController` are the known stale-index noise class — the build is the verdict); Swift **3496/373 PASSED, 0 warnings, exit 0, zero failures** (the 17 lines matching a failure-token grep are all test NAMES containing the word 'error:' plus `[measured]` printouts — neither known flake fired); npm **212/212**; both wire SHAs `shasum -c` **OK** — prediction of zero wire growth held exactly. My gate differs from theirs deliberately: it reads the LANDED position from **store ground truth** (`project.overview` clip `startBeat`), never from the seam echo, so an echo bug cannot make preview==landed vacuously true. Table verified end-to-end at raw beat 9.4: off->9.4 (raw), bar->8, beat->9, sixteenth->9.375 (all `grid`), each with the LANDED clip equal to the PREVIEWED beat and the CAUSING drop call already reporting it. Magnets verified against a deliberately OFF-GRID fixture edge placed with snap off (clip at 7.3, edges 7.3/9.3): raw 7.35 -> **7.3 `magnetClipEdge` where the grid would have said 7** (so a silent fallback to grid fails this leg); raw 7.9 (0.6 > the computed 0.25-beat radius) -> 8 `grid`; snap off -> 7.35 `raw`; and bar-1 under a sixteenth grid -> 0 `magnetBarOne` where the grid would have said 0.0625 — the exact case a nearest-wins rule kills. Artifact legs: a live drag arms the line (vacuity), the drop clears it on the CAUSING call, a post-drop update does not re-strand it, and a fresh drag previews again (so the guard is not a one-way latch). Removability re-proved independently: with `pressedMouseButtons == 0` asserted first, a `debug.arrangePointer` hover clears a standing line. 0-frame WAV now lands at 0.03125 beats instead of 0. **DISCLOSED TRADE-OFF I FOUND AND MEASURED (gate leg D1), filed not buried:** `ArrangeDropSession`'s doc promises that an `update` in `.idle` acts as an implicit enter 'so a drag whose `dropEntered` was never delivered still previews' — but after a COMPLETED drop the phase sits at `.dropped` and refuses, and nothing resets it except a delivered `enter`/`exit` (`dismissStrandedHover` resets, but requires a hover to be present, which a clean drop has already cleared). So that resilience is available only for the FIRST drag of a session. Measured as a fact, not asserted as correct — and the residual risk is NARROWER than a first reading suggests: the stranding that was actually OBSERVED (zero injection) is `enter -> update -> (stop)`, i.e. an abandoned drag, which leaves the phase at `.dragging`, NOT `.dropped`. So this gap is ORTHOGONAL to the failure mode anyone has measured: reaching it needs a COMPLETED drop followed by a WITHHELD enter, a conjunction nobody has observed and which no evidence points at. Its cost is a missing PREVIEW (the drop itself still lands correctly), against the permanent un-removable line the guard prevents. Layer (2) makes the artifact removable either way, which is why this does not gate the tick. **NOT PROVEN AND NOT CLAIMED (the m23-e staged-gate law):** that macOS delivers drag callbacks in the orders staged here, and that `NSEvent.pressedMouseButtons` reads non-zero inside the DESTINATION app during a real cross-process Finder drag — the guard that keeps the dismissal from cancelling a live preview. Both need a human with a real Finder drag. **REPORT (3) — RECORD CORRECTED 2026-07-26 BY THE m23-g ORCH PASS; THE DEFERRAL RESTED ON A WRONG PREMISE AND IS NOW WITHDRAWN.** This entry originally said the multi-item drop case was "gated on m23-g (multi-select), which is unchecked". That dependency was phantom: multi-file drop has nothing to do with multi-SELECT, and re-reading the path shows **it already fans out** — `AudioImportPlan.routesToExistingAudioTrack(fileCount:targetKind:)` at `:103` returns true only for `fileCount == 1 && targetKind == .audio`, and the else branch at `:143` already creates ONE NEW AUDIO TRACK PER FILE at the same resolved start beat.
