- [x] (m23-g2) **Group DRAG preserving relative offsets.** The geometry half of m23-g's gate. Depends on g1's selection model. **SPLIT 2026-07-26 by the orch pass — "nudge" left this title and became m23-x.** Grep-verified: the app has ZERO arrow-key handling anywhere (`leftArrow`/`rightArrow`/`upArrow`/`downArrow`/`keyboardShortcut(.left)` → no hits in `Sources/DAWApp` or `Sources/DAWAppKit`), so nudge does not exist even for ONE clip. It is a net-new keyboard surface with its own guard set and its own undo-coalescing question, and it appears NOWHERE in m23-g's gate — only in the old title. **THE PREMISE CORRECTION THAT MATTERS MOST, found by the orch pass and bigger than the beat-0 clamp: `ClipEditModel.swift:186 ClipEdit.movedStartBeat` snaps the clip's ABSOLUTE start** (`snap.snap(beat: max(0, originalStart + dragDeltaBeats))`). Applied per-clip to a group, that DESTROYS relative offsets whenever two clips sit at different sub-grid phases — a clip at beat 0 and one at beat 2.5 dragged together under `.bar` snap both land on 4, silently welding them. This — not the clamp — is what "preserving relative offsets" is actually about. **The fix shape: snap ONCE, on the ANCHOR clip (the one under the pointer), derive `effectiveDelta = snappedAnchorStart - anchorOriginalStart`, then apply that SAME delta rigidly to every selected clip.** Snapping is a property of the GESTURE, not of each clip. **Second correction — `resolveOverlap` NEVER SPLITS a stationary clip:** it returns exactly 0 or 1 clips (`[stationary]`, `[]`, or one edge-trimmed clip; its own doc says "a single trim can't punch a hole"). The earlier note about "stationary clips that `resolveOverlap` SPLITS in an early pass" was wrong — the recommended per-mover loop is still correct, and simpler than that note implied. **Third: `moveClip` is SAME-TRACK ONLY** (`locateClip(trackID:clipID:)` then mutates `tracks[t].clips[c].startBeat`; unlike `duplicateClip` there is no `toTrackId`), and the drag gesture passes the clip's own track — so vertical/cross-track drag does not exist today. g2 is HORIZONTAL-ONLY; say so rather than appearing to regress it. **THE DATA-LOSS HAZARD, resolved by the orch pass to a recommended approach the implementer must still verify rather than assume:** `ProjectStore.swift:3764 resolvingOverlaps(in:activeIDs:start:end:tempoMap:)` is the no-silent-overlap choke point (m13-b) and its `activeIDs: Set<UUID>` looks group-ready, **but that is only half true and the half that is false is the dangerous one** — it exempts the movers from trimming EACH OTHER, while `start`/`end` is a SINGLE CONTIGUOUS WINDOW applied per-track (`resolveOverlap(stationary:activeStart:activeEnd:)`, one range). A group holding clips at bar 1 and bar 9 on the same track has no single honest window, so a union `[bar1, bar9+len]` would trim clips that NO MOVER ACTUALLY OVERLAPS. **State the question precisely, because the obvious phrasing is wrong:** trimming a clip you land on top of is the store's NORMAL, INTENDED behaviour — `clip.addMIDI` already does it on ordinary single-clip adds (this was proven the hard way at g1, where it made an overlapping-clip fixture structurally unbuildable). So the g2 defect to hunt is NOT "resolvingOverlaps trims things"; it is **"a group move trims a clip no mover overlaps"** — a bug created by the union window, not a latent flaw in the choke point. **Recommended: call `resolvingOverlaps` ONCE PER MOVING CLIP with `activeIDs` = the FULL moving set, feeding the rebuilt array forward.** Each mover then contributes only its own true window, every mover is exempt in every pass, and non-contiguity dissolves; stationary clips that `resolveOverlap` SPLITS in an early pass are correctly seen as two clips by later passes. Verify this against the invariant before building on it. **Name the beat-0 clamp case explicitly** — it is what "preserves relative offsets" is actually about, and it ships broken by default: when a drag would push the leftmost clip below zero, either the WHOLE group clamps together (offsets preserved) or that one clip clamps alone (offsets silently broken). `moveClip` currently does `max(0, toStartBeat)` per clip, i.e. the broken-by-default behavior. Decide, document, and gate it. **Also decide and document the grab rule:** dragging a clip that IS in the selection moves the whole selection; dragging one that is NOT should select it first and move it alone (otherwise a stale selection silently drags clips the user is not touching). Route: swift-app-engineer. GATE: FIXTURE-LAW computed, not discovered — place clips at known beats, drag by a known delta, assert each landed at exactly `start + delta`; **the DISCRIMINATOR leg for the snapping bug, which every on-grid fixture would miss: put one clip ON the grid and another deliberately OFF it (e.g. beats 0 and 2.5) under `.bar` snap, drag together, and assert the 2.5-beat gap SURVIVES — a per-clip-snap implementation welds them and must fail this leg;** a 3-clip move is one `edit.history` step; a mixed-track selection preserves cross-track offsets; the beat-0 clamp behaves as documented; a NON-CONTIGUOUS same-track selection leaves the innocent clip between the movers untrimmed and its notes intact (the hazard above, as a data-loss guard in the m23-e E8 tradition); suites. — **DONE 2026-07-26 (swift-app-engineer).** Shipped as `ProjectStore.moveClips(ids:byBeats:)` (DELTA-based, not absolute-target — a rigid translation is then a TYPE-LEVEL fact rather than a caller promise, and m23-x's nudge is a thin layer over the same verb), `ArrangeGroupDrag.plan` (DAWAppKit, headless: snap the ANCHOR's absolute start ONCE, derive the rigid delta), `AppModel.dragArrangeClips` (the ONE handler the ClipBlock gesture AND the new seam both run), and `debug.arrangeDrag {clipId, deltaBeats, snap}` — **debug tier, ZERO wire growth; Commands.swift and server.ts byte-identical by SHA at close-out** (`bc5eb236…`/`c588b614…`, both untouched by MTIME too). **THREE HOMES, ONE EACH, so divergence is unrepresentable (the m23-f lesson):** `ClipEdit.movedStartBeat` = the ONE anchor-snap; `ArrangeGroupDrag.plan` = the ONE rigid-delta producer; `ProjectStore.moveClips` = the ONE WHOLE-GROUP clamp AND the ONE producer of positions. The gesture layer never produces the GROUP's final positions, so there is nothing for it to agree with by luck — but read the next sentence before concluding it is clamp-free, because it is not. **Stated precisely, because the first version of this record and the code comment both overclaimed it and that cost a review round: the gesture layer is NOT clamp-free.** `ClipEdit.movedStartBeat` floors the ANCHOR at `max(0, …)` before snapping, so a drag whose anchor is asked below beat 0 has already had that reduction absorbed there. That is not a second computation of the group clamp — `max(delta, -minStartAmongSelection)` over the selection and a floor on the anchor alone are DIFFERENT QUANTITIES — but it does mean the reduction happens in one of two places depending on which clip the pointer grabbed. **PREMISE CHECK — all three orch corrections HELD** (per-clip snap is the real defect, `resolveOverlap` never splits, `moveClip` is same-track-only). **`moveClip` was deliberately NOT reimplemented on top of `moveClips`**: `current + (max(0,target) − current) != max(0,target)` in floating point, and it is a live wire verb with exact-start-beat tests. Instead `moveClipsLabel`/`moveClipsKey` make a ONE-clip group journal `"Move Clip '<name>'"` / `clip.move:<uuid>` VERBATIM, so the arrange can route EVERY drag through the group verb with no count==1 branch and no journal regression (proven: it coalesces with a subsequent `moveClip`). **FOUR FINDINGS worth carrying forward.** (1) **ORDER-INDEPENDENCE IS NOT FREE.** For mutually-disjoint movers the per-mover passes are genuinely order-independent (trims are monotone), and a mutation removing the sort passed 35/35 — the test suite could not see it. It is FALSE for movers that overlap EACH OTHER, which only a sanctioned m11-d crossfade pair produces: resident [0,10) with movers landing [10,13) and [12,14) survives as [14,20) in one order and is REMOVED in the other. Passes are now SORTED by landing start, which makes the result deterministic (not principled), and a model-level overlapping-mover test now kills that mutant. (2) **A ZERO-DELTA GUARD IS LOAD-BEARING, not an optimisation** — a live drag emits many sub-grid-line events, and running the overlap pass at the clip's CURRENT position would trim a legitimately-overlapping crossfade partner for a gesture that moved nothing. (3) **`presentArrangeSplitRefusal` spawns a 6-second auto-dismiss `Task` PER CALL**; a drag calls per pointer event, so a refused group drag would have spawned hundreds of sleeping tasks for one bubble — guarded to fire only on a NEW message. (4) **`onSelectionRendered` fires on `.onChange(of: selection)` and a group MOVE changes no selection**, so waiting on `arrangeSelectionRenderSeq` after a drag would ALWAYS time out and, worse, would read whatever the last selection change left behind. A separate `arrangeClipLayoutRenderSeq` (cheap clip-only hash, deliberately not `.onChange(of: tracks)`) is the seam's liveness signal. **GATE (final, after the round-2 echo fix).** Swift **3556/377** 0-warn, 69 s, **sole failure the KNOWN SoundBank T1 AU-preparation flake** — verbatim signature (`Audio Unit preparation timed out after 10.0 seconds`), isolated **7/7 in 0.593 s**, and it fired in BOTH of round 2's full runs while staying quiet in round 1's, which is the documented load-sensitivity, not a regression (nothing in this item touches AU hosting). Round 1 was 3553/377 fully green. + npm **212/212** (13 s, incl. the tempo-lint — run even though `server.ts` is SHA-identical, because "should be green" is not the standard used anywhere else in this cycle; not re-run for round 2, which touches no TypeScript) + staging **45/45** on 17695 (38 in round 1, +7 in P8) (`scripts/gates/m23g2-group-drag.mjs`, torn down PIDFILE-EXACT; the gate spawns `.build/debug/DAWApp` — the same binary `swift run DAWApp` execs — and drove 38 legs through its control port, which is a stronger launch proof than launch-and-quit). **SIX MUTATIONS, each caught by exactly the right tests** (`per-clip-clamp` → 5 red; `union-window` → 3 red incl. both non-contiguous legs; `non-incremental-delta` → 3 red; `no-coalescing-key` → 3 red; `split-atomicity` → 2 red; `unsorted-passes` → 1 red, and ONLY after finding 1 above); sources restored byte-identically by SHA. Headline discriminator lives in `DAWAppKitTests` as a COMPOSITION, because neither piece alone can see the weld — with a permanent VALIDITY LEG running the rejected per-clip implementation and asserting it welds 0/2.5 onto beat 4 and shreds a clip from 2 beats to 0.5. **GATE-ARITHMETIC CONFESSION (FIXTURE LAW, violated three times in one file):** the first gate run was 33/37 and ALL FOUR failures were my arithmetic, not the app — `round(1.6/4)*4 = 0` so a 1.6 raw delta made a whole leg vacuous; a staged drag RE-ANCHORS from rest so three drags are +2.5/+4/+4, not +12; nearest-grid on a 1/16 grid sends 16.1 to 16.125, not 16.0625. **BEHAVIOUR WORTH KNOWING, found by the 4th failure and NOT a defect:** a near-zero drag translation on an OFF-GRID clip is not a zero move — the anchor snaps to the grid and the whole group follows. That is `ClipEdit.movedStartBeat`'s pre-g2 single-clip behaviour preserved, and offsets survive it; the gate's zero-delta control now grid-aligns the anchor first. **ROUND-2 FIX — the ECHO defect independent verification caught, and the one real bug of the item.** `ArrangeGroupDragOutcome.clamped` was the STORE's flag alone, which made it **ANCHOR-DEPENDENT**: clips at 4 and 12, both selected, dragged −10 with snap off land at 0/8 with effective delta −4 **either way**, but grabbing the LEFTMOST reported `clamped:false` and grabbing the RIGHTMOST reported `clamped:true`. Same geometry, same effective delta, opposite report — because the gesture floor had already absorbed the reduction in the first case, so the store was asked for exactly what it could do and answered honestly. Behaviour was always correct; only the ECHO lied. Worth fixing rather than filing under the ECHO-SEAM LAW (m23-e: a misleading echo poisons every diagnosis made through it) and because `clamped` is the instrument m23-x's nudge inherits. **FIX:** `ArrangeGroupDrag.Plan` gained `flooredAtZero` — a RESTATEMENT of `movedStartBeat`'s own `max(0, originalStart + drag)` predicate, not a second guess at it — and `clamped` became a COMPUTED union `storeClamped || gestureFlooredAtZero` (computed so the two stages cannot drift from it), with both stage flags echoed alongside so `requested == effective` on a floored drag reads as an explanation instead of a paradox. **THE OVER-CORRECTION THAT WAS AVOIDED, and now has its own guard:** the tempting predicate is "the landing ended up lower than asked", which would report a clamp for every DOWNWARD SNAP — and the item's own test 4 shows why that is wrong (anchor 6, raw −5, `.bar`: `max(0,1)` = 1, which SNAPS to 0; the grid did that, not a floor). Legs 14/P8f pin it FALSE. **`requestedDeltaBeats` remains anchor-dependent BY DESIGN** (−4 vs −10 for that repro): it means "what the STORE was asked for", matching `ClipsMoveResult`'s field of the same name, and shadowing it with a different app-layer meaning is precisely the drift this cycle was spent designing out — `gestureFlooredAtZero` sitting next to it is what makes it interpretable. Gate phase **P8 (7 legs, two INDEPENDENT fixtures on two tracks rather than one undone and redone)** + DAWAppKit legs 13/14/15; the mutation reverting `clamped` to the store flag alone reproduced the reported defect verbatim (`leftGrab.clamped=false rightGrab.clamped=true`) and took P8c/P8g red while every control stayed green. **The Swift suite is nearly SILENT on this fix by construction** — `ArrangeGroupDragOutcome` lives in `DAWApp` and is unreachable from `DAWAppKitTests`, so legs 13–15 deliberately pin the two INPUTS and refuse to recompute the union (a test-local `a || b` would be a second producer of the quantity under test, free to agree by luck); the app's actual wiring is proven ONLY by P8. **CLAMP-VS-SNAP, decided and documented in code:** offsets WIN — the leftmost lands on exactly 0 and every gap survives, even though the anchor may then sit off-grid; `clamped`/`requestedDeltaBeats`/`effectiveDeltaBeats` report it, and the drag readout now shows the ACHIEVED start (it previously showed the requested one, which is precisely the value the clamp overrides). **GRAB RULE:** dragging a selected clip moves the whole selection; dragging an unselected one routes through `clickClip(id:modifiers:[])` (so `arrangeKeyFocusNonce` still bumps and DELETE still reaches the arrange) and moves it alone. **HORIZONTAL ONLY** — cross-track drag does not exist and was not added. **NOT PROVEN, recorded as SKIP not as a pass:** the comp-member refusal end-to-end (a take group needs ≥2 OVERLAPPING clips and `clip.addMIDI` routes through `resolvingOverlaps` → UNBUILDABLE from the wire, m23-g1 law; covered headless instead), and real `DragGesture` translation delivery (`DragGesture.Value` has no public initializer — no seam can synthesize one); and a MIXED-TRACK move where BOTH tracks hold residents that get trimmed in the SAME edit (P5b proves cross-track offsets survive, but its second track is resident-free — the per-track grouping makes the risk low, not zero, and no leg exercises it). **PRE-EXISTING, NOT INTRODUCED AND NOT FIXED:** moving a crossfaded clip by a non-zero delta still trims its partner — `moveClip` has always done this; g2 inherits it unchanged. **And the zero-delta guard is an EXACT float compare, not an epsilon:** `startBeat = old + (snapped − old)` need not reproduce `snapped` bit-exactly, so a later drag event can compute a ~1e-16 delta, slip past the guard and run the overlap pass — which for a crossfade pair is the same partner trim. Sub-case of the line above, no new defect class; left alone deliberately rather than tuned by guesswork.
- [x] (m23-g3) **Rubber-band (marquee) selection in the arrange.** **SPLIT 2026-07-26 by the orch pass: track multi-select moved OUT to m23-y**, because m23-g3's gate covers ONLY the marquee — track selection had no gate leg at all and carries an unanswered semantics question, and bundling an ungated semantics change with a net-new gesture is exactly what the g-split exists to prevent. Still deliberately last: **shift/⌘ click alone is enough to select 3 clips, and therefore enough to satisfy m23-g's entire gate** — this is convenience layered on a working mechanism, the same shape as m23-e→m23-v. **PREMISE VERIFIED, and it HOLDS: net-new confirmed** — zero `marquee`/`rubber band`/`lasso`/`selectionRect` anywhere in `Sources/` (the only hits are `VibeMeterModel`'s unrelated colour-ramp comments), and `ArrangeSelection.swift:66-68` explicitly reserved "the honest 2-D range gesture" for this item. WHERE IT LIVES: `pointerHoverSurface` (`TimelineLanesView.swift:1276`) — the full-bleed clear `Rectangle` beneath everything interactive, already the home for empty-space gestures. So `floor(y / rowHeight)` — or any uniform pitch — is WRONG for any project with an expanded automation row or a takes section, and RIGHT for every default project, which is precisely why a naive fixture cannot see it. Reuse `laneTop`; never re-derive the ladder. **HAZARD 2, the likeliest regression and it touches code this change never edits:** `pointerHoverSurface` already carries TWO tap gestures whose declaration ORDER was chosen deliberately (`:1287-1297` — higher count first, chosen OVER `ExclusiveGesture` because that composition delays every seek). A `DragGesture` added to the same surface can break click-to-seek (m17-c #3) or double-click-create (m23-e) without either code path being touched. GATE — legs, with the arithmetic done BEFORE the fixture (FIXTURE LAW): (1) horizontal correctness — a band across empty space selects exactly the intersected clips (known clip rects, a band chosen to include two and exclude a third); (2) **the vertical DISCRIMINATOR, computed: 4 tracks, `ui.showAutomation {trackId}` on track 0 (verified to add the 64pt row on `isExpanded` ALONE — no lane needed, `extraHeight` and `automationLanes` agree on that condition). Real tops become 0 / 104 / 144 / 184 at the default 34+6 pitch, so a band spanning y∈[150,170] intersects ONLY track 2's lane [144,178) while the uniform-division reading lands in track 3 — the two id sets DIFFER, which is the whole point of the leg. `rowHeight` is USER-ADJUSTABLE (beta m10-d), so the seam MUST return the resolved lane tops and the gate MUST compute the band from those, never from the constants above**; (3) additive with shift; (4) the band asserted POSITIVELY mid-drag — rect non-nil with the expected geometry — because "gone after" alone passes vacuously against an implementation that never draws it; (5) **click-to-seek and double-click-create both still work AFTER the marquee lands** (pre-existing behaviours with existing seams; this is the regression the change actually threatens); (6) suites. **STAGED-GATE LAW applies: a `debug.arrangeMarquee` seam never fires a tap first, so it cannot see a drag that begins-then-cancels into a stray seek. Drive at least one leg through the real tap-then-drag ordering, or record the gap explicitly in the close record rather than let it pass implicitly.** Route: `ui-design-engineer`. — **DONE 2026-07-26 (ui-design-engineer).** Shipped as `DAWAppKit.ArrangeMarquee` (headless: `band(from:to:)` normalizes, `hits(band:lanes:pixelsPerBeat:)` intersects, `laneIndices` for the row question — the ONE band-to-ids home), two new mutators ON `ArrangeSelection` (`replace(with:)` / `formUnion(_:)`, `private(set)` untouched), `AppModel.applyArrangeMarquee` (the ONE handler the `DragGesture` and the seam share), and `debug.arrangeMarquee {act:"begin"|"changed"|"end", x, y, shift?|command?}` — **debug tier, ZERO wire growth; `Commands.swift` and `server.ts` verified byte-identical by `shasum -c` at close-out** (`bc5eb236…`/`c588b614…`). **THE HAZARD-1 DEFENCE IS STRUCTURAL, not careful:** `ArrangeMarquee` takes the RESOLVED lane bands as input and has no notion of a row pitch, so there is nothing in it that COULD re-derive the ladder; the view builds them from `laneTop(i)` exactly as `pointerLanes` does. The seam echoes `laneTops` + `rowHeight` on a BARE read (before any drag exists), which is what lets a gate COMPUTE its fixture instead of hardcoding 34/6/64. **FOCUS DECISION, stated because the brief's "adopt when there is none" is under-determined for a SET: a band NEVER adopts a focus.** A band has no anchor clip (`ArrangeClickIntent`'s own doc records that arrange clips are a 2-D field with no canonical order), so promoting an arbitrary member would open the piano roll on a clip the user never clicked — and, because the marquee applies LIVE, would flicker the editor clip-to-clip mid-sweep. (2) **A SKIPPING LEG IS A PASSING GATE.** The pixel legs originally SKIPPED when the resting capture was not bit-stable, and the very first calibration run passed 36/36 against a build whose band was never drawn — the reported rect survives deleting the visual. The stability control is now RETRIED, and failing to establish one is a FAILED leg, never a skip. (3) **RESTORING THE SOURCE IS NOT RESTORING THE BINARY.** The calibration script restored files, verified SHAs, and left the last mutant `.build/debug/DAWApp` in place; the next "clean" gate run tested the mutant and failed Z1, costing a debugging round before the cause was found. It now rebuilds after every restore. **STATED COVERAGE GAP, not a passing leg: SwiftUI's real gesture ARBITRATION is unproven.** The staged pointer seam calls `handleEmptyClick`/`handleDoubleClick` directly, so R1/R2 prove the handlers are reachable and correct, not that a physical mouse tap still reaches them past the new `DragGesture`; a real drag is not injectable either (`DragGesture.Value` has no public initializer, and the staging binary has no Accessibility grant). R3 DOES drive the real tap-THEN-drag ORDERING the STAGED-GATE LAW asks for (click seeks the playhead onto the band's origin, `zone` is asserted to read `playhead-grab` so the leg cannot be vacuous, then the band arms from that exact point). The remaining gap needs a human with a mouse. **SECOND STATED GAP, same family, equally unobservable: the 250 ms click-suppression window lives IN THE TAP CLOSURE, which the staged path never runs — so no gate leg can observe it in either direction, and a leg written for it could only pass vacuously.** It is now armed only for a band that actually swept area (`if band.width > 0 || band.height > 0`), so a degenerate release cannot swallow the user's next click for a gesture that drew and selected nothing; that narrowing is REASONED, not measured, and is recorded in the code at the assignment. **ORCH INDEPENDENT VERIFICATION 2026-07-26 — tick CONFIRMED, and the calibration produced the finding that matters.** Orch gate **18/18** (`scratchpad/orch-m23g3.mjs`, written BEFORE the implementation so it tests what was ASKED). Seam-contract note, NOT a defect: the seam keys its phase as `act` with `shift`/`command` booleans — the house convention (`debug.arrangeSelection`/`debug.arrangePointer`), while the brief only said "a begin/changed/end progression". The gate was adapted to the shipped contract; no assertion was weakened. **CALIBRATION: 18/18 → 17/18 under a mutation replacing `laneTop(i)` with a uniform `i*(rowHeight+laneSpacing)` in `marqueeLanes`, and EXACTLY leg B1 went red — no collateral, the discriminator caught precisely the bug it exists for.** Source restored BY COPY (never `git checkout` in this tree) and verified byte-identical, SHA `bdf0079fa3076c0b93b621d6042919c00b553913ca8bfff58b6dffcf8644707c`. **THE REUSABLE FINDING — the 15 headless `ArrangeMarqueeTests` CANNOT SEE that mutation.** The full suite was run against the mutated build and its ONLY failures were the known SoundBank T1 flake; every marquee unit test passed while the shipping app selected the WRONG TRACK. (Precision on that flake claim: the 7/7-in-0.595 s isolation was measured against the RESTORED binary, not the mutant. The identification still holds — the mutation is three lines of arrange-view geometry the AU-preparation path never reaches — but it is an inference from location, not a measurement under the mutation, and is recorded as the weaker of the two.) That is not a gap in those tests — it is the shadow of the design's own strength: making `ArrangeMarquee` structurally unable to re-derive the ladder moves the hazard OUT of the type and INTO ITS CALLER, where only an integration gate reaches it. **Generalise (HARDENING-SHADOW LAW): when a type is hardened by taking resolved inputs, its unit tests stop being able to fail for the hazard that hardening was FOR, and the gate that replaces them must be built deliberately — not assumed redundant.** **This is the SAME FAMILY as m23-d's TEST-DOUBLE LAW ("a double that records EVENTS proves routing, never sound"), and naming the family is the point so a third cycle does not rediscover it: in both, THE THING YOU HARDENED OR STUBBED IS NO LONGER THE THING THAT CAN FAIL — `EventCaptureInstrument` structurally cannot observe a skipped render, `ArrangeMarquee` structurally cannot observe a wrong ladder. Whenever a design makes a hazard unrepresentable INSIDE a boundary, ask immediately where the hazard MOVED TO, and gate THAT.** Two honest limits on this gate, recorded rather than glossed: legs D1/E1 assert band geometry through `CGRect` accessors, which the implementer measured to be **invariant to a missing normalization** (see their `CGRect`-standardizes-everything finding), so they verify the CLAIM "a reversed drag selects the same clips" but cannot catch a missing `abs()`; and the real tap-then-drag SwiftUI arbitration remains unproven from any seam (STAGED-GATE LAW) — F1/F2 prove the seek and create HANDLERS survived, not that a physical tap still reaches them. **THIRD GAP, closed as a STATIC READ and tallied as the weaker evidence it is (UNBUILDABLE-FIXTURE precedent): my X1 leg proves the SEAM's echo agrees with `ArrangeSelection`, never that the LIVE gesture routes through the same handler rather than a parallel one — the m23-e echo-seam class, one level up. (2) A single sampled gap → **25/25 → 24/25 with EXACTLY F1 red**, returning 191 against the correct 142 — byte-identical to the uniform reading F0b predicts — **while A1 stayed GREEN.** That pair is the measured proof that the INDEX hazard and the GAP hazard need DIFFERENT FIXTURES: the bus-between-channels shape cannot see the gap bug, and the one-channel/two-bus shape cannot see the index bug. Sources restored BY COPY, `shasum -c` OK, rebuilt, and the full suite re-measured after the LAST restore. **THE FINDING THAT CORRECTS MY OWN m23-h WARNING: I filed the divider-gap hazard with the WRONG FIXTURE SHAPE.** I wrote that the mixer's divider breaks `TrackRowLadder.spacing`; it does — but not where I said. Because the partition is a VIEW of the array, a reorder never changes the channel/bus COUNTS, so the divider stays at the same slot boundary and every visually-effective drag is a permutation WITHIN one section. The divider gap is therefore never the VACATED gap, and on any channels-first project sampling slot 0-1 is accidentally RIGHT. The hazard is reachable only when slot 0-1 is ITSELF the divider gap — **exactly one channel and at least two buses**. My mid-flight instruction to assert 'the vacated slot at the divider boundary is 10 + dividerWidth + 10' would have gone RED AGAINST CORRECT CODE; the implementer caught it and the gate I had already written independently asserts the correct direction. **A SECOND DIRECTION RULE FELL OUT OF IT:** `vacatedSlot` cannot be `gap(after: from)` either — the surviving gap is the plain one on the side facing the DESTINATION, and the implementer's third calibration showed the non-directional read also turns the ARRANGE's existing m23-h test red (`gap(after: last)` is 0). **SEMANTICS HELD, and one open decision was DECLINED rather than smuggled in:** I nearly shipped section-clamping (a channel may only land among channels) to kill the degenerate case where dropping a channel on a bus slot changes the array but nothing on screen. That is kind-based reachability — a second ordering rule the arrange does not have, i.e. the exact failure mode this line's semantics decision rejected. The real defect was narrower: `moves` was computed on ARRAY indices while the user looks at VISUAL ones. Redefining it on the visual order kills the degenerate case with no new rule, and the implementer went one better — `ResolvedStripDrop.landing` is an OPTIONAL struct carrying index AND indicatorX together, so 'there is a line' and 'there is an index to commit' are ONE fact that cannot drift. **CLAMPING REMAINS THE USER'S CALL** if they want it. **A DECISION MY BRIEF DID NOT COVER, found and pinned by the implementer:** when a landing CROSSES the divider (dragging the second of two buses onto a channel's slot is visually effective — the buses reverse — but the strip lands back in the bus section), the line is drawn at the RESULTING slot, not the pointer's; the two coincide for every within-section drag, so it needed its own test or a later refactor to 'pointer slot' would be silently green. **CROSS-SURFACE SEAM CONTRACT, found by running the gate:** BOTH staging seams refuse unless their own surface is on screen — the console only measures itself while raised, and `debug.trackHeaderDrag` symmetrically refuses with empty `rowTops` while the Mix tab is up. The implementer deliberately did NOT flip `workspaceMode` inside the seam (a debug READ that switches the user's window is a mutation in disguise) and echoes it instead, so an empty ladder is diagnosable; the gate sequences `ui.showMixer` around each leg. Both refusals are model ones — they name what they need. **TWO ERRORS OF MINE IN THE GATE, both red against correct code:** C2 read `edit.history.undo.at(-1)` when the stack is MOST-RECENT-FIRST (it asserted against `Add Track 'C0'`), and E1 compared raw UUIDs across two fixtures that each mint fresh ids — it could never have been equal. Both fixed by asserting the field/axis actually verified (GATE-ASSERTION LAW); nothing weakened. **STATED GAPS, never tallied as passes:** real mouse gesture arbitration is unproven (the seam drives the handlers directly, and every other mixer control is a `DragGesture(minimumDistance: 0)` with a `contextMenu` on the strip, so the name-row handle could be dead in the user's hands while all 25 legs stay green — not injectable without an Accessibility grant, STAGED-GATE LAW); and the landing is asserted at horizontal scroll 0 only, where a `.local`-space mistake is silent.
- [x] (m23-i) **Unsaved-change visibility + close confirmation — CLOSED 2026-07-26, NO ACTION, by user decision.** Filed as a decision rather than a bug because verification showed nothing was broken: the dirty flag exists, clean quit already autosaves (`autosaveIfNeeded()` on `willTerminate`, `DAWProApp.swift:740`), AND the window title already marks unsaved state — `ContentView.swift:259` appends `" — Edited"` from `store.isDirty`. The user found that marker in use and judged it sufficient ("I think we are good there, I later noticed it says 'Edited' which is good for unsave mark"), so the close-confirmation modal is deliberately NOT built: it would sit on top of both a visible indicator and a working autosave backstop. Reopen only if unsaved work is ever actually lost.
- [ ] (m23-j) **Kill the Keychain password prompt: give the bundle a stable signing identity. — DEFERRED by the user 2026-07-26 ("let's keep it open for now, no action"): filed with the diagnosis complete, NOT scheduled; `/dev-cycle` must skip it until the user says otherwise.** Verified root cause — `scripts/bundle.sh:75` runs `codesign --force --sign -` (ad-hoc). An ad-hoc signature is regenerated on every build, so the Keychain item's ACL no longer matches the requesting binary and macOS re-prompts; `kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlock` (`KeyStore.swift:188`) governs device-lock state, not the ACL, so it is not implicated and needs no change. Fix: sign the bundle with a STABLE identity — a locally created self-signed cert is sufficient for dev and needs no Apple enrollment — and keep the bundle id fixed so "Always Allow" sticks across rebuilds. Real Apple Developer enrollment would also solve it and unblock pkg-b/c/e (pending user decision, see Blocked). Route: swift-app-engineer + docs. GATE: two consecutive rebuild-and-launch cycles with zero prompts, documented cert-creation steps, `.env` dev path left working unchanged.
- [x] (m23-k) **Standard MIDI File import/export per track — SPLIT 2026-07-26 by the orch pass into k1 → k2 → k3 → k4; this umbrella line stays for context and is ticked only when all four are.** Confirmed absent — zero SMF/`.mid` handling anywhere in `Sources/` or `mcp-server/src/`, so today only audio can be imported (`AudioLaneDrop`). The whole capability is SMF type 0/1 read and write in DAWCore (UI-free, headless-testable): tempo/meter map, note on/off with velocity, CC/pitch-bend/channel-pressure (the controller-lane model already exists), multi-track split to one DAW track per MIDI track, drag-drop plus menu paths, and the inverse export per track and for the whole project, with a full triple for the new commands (`clip.importMIDI`/`project.importMIDI` + `render.exportMIDI` or equivalent), additive at END. **WHY THE SPLIT:** the original single line bundled a high-risk binary parser, its inverse, a project-mapping decision that needs a `daw-architect` read, two wire triples and a UI path — four different kinds of risk, only one of which is the parser. **THE SPLIT'S LOAD-BEARING DECISION, and the reason k1 comes first alone: k1's intermediate representation is TICK-NATIVE.** SMF measures time in ticks against a per-file division; `MIDINote` measures it in beats relative to a clip. If k1's IR stored beats it would have already made the tempo-map mapping decision that k3 is supposed to get the architect read on, and k3 could then never be gated independently. So k1 is a faithful readout of the bytes and nothing more — the `SoundFontPresetReader` posture this codebase already models ("stays a faithful readout of the bytes"). **FIXTURE DOCTRINE FOR ALL FOUR (established by a spike this cycle, and it is the m23-z ONE HAZARD PER FIXTURE law transferred from geometry to parsing):** Apple's `AudioToolbox` `MusicSequence` writes valid SMF (`MusicSequenceFileCreate`, verified: `MThd` len 6, format 1, 3 tracks, 480 tpqn, 167 bytes) and `MusicSequenceFileLoad` reads it — a genuinely third-party encoder AND decoder to check ours against. But Apple's output is ONE SHAPE, and it is exactly the shape that omits every classic parser hazard: no running status, no note-on-velocity-0-as-note-off, format 1 rather than 0, no SMPTE division, no long multi-byte VLQ. So Apple fixtures gate the SHAPE and hand-authored spec bytes gate the HAZARDS; neither substitutes for the other. **LAW L9 HOLDS THROUGHOUT — `AudioToolbox` appears only in scratchpad generators and gate scripts, NEVER in `Sources/DAWCore`**, which parses with pure Foundation `Data` like `SoundFontPresetReader` does. Route: swift-app-engineer (codec in DAWCore) with a daw-architect read on the tempo-map mapping at k3. **CHANGELOG CONVENTION FOR ALL FOUR (set at k1): there is ONE entry, `## In progress — MIDI file support (.mid)`, holding a four-box checklist. k2/k3/k4 EDIT that block — tick their box and expand its line — they do NOT add a new dated entry. Four stacked "nothing to try yet" headers above thirteen usable items is the failure mode this avoids; at k4 the block loses the "In progress" title and becomes the release note.** — **UMBRELLA TICKED 2026-07-27: all FIVE sub-items are done (k1, k2, k3, k4a, k4b — the line says "all four" because it was written before k4 was itself split into k4a domain+wire and k4b UI).** MIDI file support is now reachable WITHOUT an agent: drag a `.mid` onto an arrange lane, or File -> Import MIDI… / Export MIDI…. L9 held throughout — `AudioToolbox` never entered `Sources/DAWCore`; it appears only in scratchpad generators and gate scripts, where it served as the third-party arbiter for both k1's reader and k4a's writer. **The fixture doctrine paid for itself twice:** Apple fixtures gated the SHAPE, hand-authored spec bytes gated the HAZARDS, and neither would have caught what the other did. **Known open, filed not buried:** program change does not reach the exported bytes (k2 is byte-frozen; the IR carries it and the report says so), and a failed DROP is silent while the menu entries alert.
- [x] (m23-k1) **SMF decoder: read the bytes, faithfully and tick-natively.** The parser half, DAWCore-only, zero wire and zero UI. Chunk walk (`MThd`/`MTrk`, unknown chunks skipped by length per spec rather than treated as errors), variable-length quantities, **running status**, note on/off pairing including **note-on with velocity 0 as a note-off**, CC / pitch-bend / channel-pressure, and the meta events that matter (set-tempo, time-signature, track name, end-of-track). Both formats: **type 1** (one part per `MTrk`) and **type 0** (one track carrying all 16 channels, which the decoder must split by channel — the two differ in structure, not just a header field). Output is a tick-native IR: ticks plus the file's division verbatim, no beats, no tempo applied, no project types touched. Every read bounds-checked so a truncated or malformed file yields a teaching error, never a crash and never a plausible-looking empty parse. Route: swift-app-engineer. GATE: **(a)** Apple-generated fixtures parse to exactly the notes/CCs/tempo events they were built from — checked in as BYTES under `Tests/`, generated by a scratchpad tool that is deliberately NOT part of the build, so a later cycle cannot regenerate them with our own writer and silently evaporate the independence; **(b)** hand-authored spec-byte fixtures cover each hazard Apple's encoder omits, one hazard per fixture; **(c)** each hazard fixture is shown DISCRIMINATING — a naive reader that assumes every event carries a status byte, or that treats note-on-velocity-0 as a note-on, must fail the fixture that targets it, or the fixture is not testing anything; **(d)** malformed/truncated/absent-chunk inputs give teaching errors; suites. — **DONE 2026-07-26 (swift-app-engineer), ORCH-VERIFIED.** Shipped: `Sources/DAWCore/StandardMIDIFile.swift` (the tick-native IR — `SMFFormat`, `SMFDivision` with `.ticksPerQuarterNote`/`.smpte(fps:ticksPerFrame:)`, `SMFNote`, `SMFControllerEvent` reusing the existing `MIDIControllerType`, `SMFTempoEvent`, `SMFTimeSignatureEvent`, `SMFTrack`, `SMFWarning`, `SMFDecodeError`), `Sources/DAWCore/StandardMIDIFileReader.swift` (parser + a bounds-checked `SMFCursor`), 39 tests / 1 suite, and `resources: [.copy("Fixtures")]` on `DAWCoreTests` — `.copy` not `.process`, deliberately, because these fixtures are binary evidence and `.process` reserves the right to transform them. **The tick-native constraint held**: no beats anywhere in the IR, tempo stored as microseconds-per-quarter verbatim, so the tempo-map mapping decision is still entirely k3's to make with the architect. Tempo/meter are hoisted to FILE level (global by spec; format 0 has no honest per-channel answer) with each event keeping a `sourceTrackIndex`, so the hoist discards nothing. **Three spec ambiguities decided and pinned rather than left implicit:** (1) a re-struck pitch pairs **FIFO** — LIFO can starve a pitch held under a trill by letting newer strikes steal its note-off — pinned by a test that also asserts against the LIFO answer; (2) note-ons still open at end-of-track are **closed at `endTick` and warned, not dropped**, because dropping is the same plausible-empty-parse failure this decoder exists to prevent, just smaller; (3) a missing `FF 2F 00` is a **refusal**, since the marker is the only thing distinguishing "the writer forgot it" from "the file was cut off mid-phrase", and in the second case the recovered notes are a fragment presented as a whole song (matches Apple; no lenient knob was added, on the grounds that an untested code path is worse than a missing one and adding it later is additive). **DISCRIMINATION PROOF, 7 mutations × 39 tests, real counts:** M1 assume-every-event-carries-a-status-byte → **3** red (its hazard + 2 inline running-status tests); M2 velocity-0-is-a-note-on → **1**; M3 stop-VLQ-after-2-bytes → **1**; M4 ignore-the-channel-nibble-on-type-0 → **4** (its hazard + 3 inline format-0 split tests); M5 swap-pitch-bend-byte-order → **1**; M6 error-on-unknown-chunks → **1**; M7 read-division-as-unsigned-tpqn → **1**. Every hazard reddened under its own mutation and none stayed green; M1/M4's splash is onto tests of the SAME rule, which is the right kind. (The implementer chose "stop VLQ after 2 bytes" over "one byte per delta" because the latter reddens five fixtures indiscriminately — two-byte deltas are everywhere.) **And the load-bearing result: `apple-type1.mid` stayed GREEN under all seven mutations** — a decoder gated only on a real third-party encoder's output would have shipped all seven bugs. **ORCH INDEPENDENT VERIFICATION (my runs, not theirs):** Swift **3642/383 → 3681/384** (+39 tests, +1 suite — exactly the new suite), 71.1 s, the ONLY failure being the documented pre-existing `SoundBankHostingTests` T1 AU-preparation flake (3 issues out of one dead render), which **isolates 7/7 in 0.583 s**; note it fired in MY run and in NEITHER of the implementer's, which is one more data point for memory's "nondeterministic, isolate before believing it" reading — and it fired at load **8.87**. **Zero warnings on a FORCED `swift build --build-tests` recompile** (`touch` on the manifest + a new source), which matters because the implementer found that the plain `test.sh` log does not reliably surface build warnings; the `resources:` line also REMOVED a pre-existing unhandled-resource warning for the 14 fixture files. Wire **154 commands / 157 MCP tools — UNCHANGED and byte-identical** (`shasum -c` OK on `Commands.swift` and `server.ts`): this item ships ZERO wire surface BY DESIGN, the one recent item to do so, because there is nothing user-facing to command until k3. LAW L9 verified: `Sources/DAWCore` imports only `Foundation` and `Observation`; `AudioToolbox`/`CoreMIDI` appear nowhere but in comments. **ALL 13 FIXTURE SHA-256 MATCH THE PINS I TOOK BEFORE DELEGATING** — `apple-type1 019c62ea`, `hazard-controllers 100ef221`, `hazard-note-on-vel0 465397bd`, `hazard-running-status 0575b50e`, `hazard-smpte-division 979ac37d`, `hazard-type0-multichannel e24511e0`, `hazard-unknown-chunk 689d38f9`, `hazard-vlq-multibyte cf0205f5`, `malformed-bad-magic cb21e94f`, `malformed-empty e3b0c442`, `malformed-no-end-of-track fc1c3183`, `malformed-truncated-header 7ed5302a`, `malformed-truncated-track 3a5e40b6` — so the parser was made to match the expectation table and not the table to the parser, which is the whole point of the orchestrator generating the fixtures. A test asserts those hashes from the bundle, which also proves the resources actually bundle, so the fixture legs cannot pass green-by-absence. **MY OWN INDEPENDENT EXERCISE — A FIXTURE THE PARSER HAS NEVER SEEN:** I generated a fresh SMF with Apple's encoder differing from every checked-in fixture in each dimension that could hide a bug — division **96** rather than 480 (so a hardcoded 480 surfaces), channels 3 and 9 rather than 0 and 1, off-grid onsets (beats 0.25/1.75/3.5), velocities 7 and 127 at the range edges, a pitch bend, and two CC numbers — with the expectation table printed from the WRITE inputs, never from a parse. A scratchpad probe (separate SPM package, path dependency, zero repo residue) fed it to `StandardMIDIFileReader`: **23/23**, exact on ticks, lengths, velocities, channels, both tempos, both CCs, and pitch bend 2048 decoded LSB-first. **CALIBRATED so its green is not vacuous:** the same expectations against the checked-in 480-tpqn fixture give **3/23**, so the probe genuinely reads the file rather than asserting constants (the 3 survivors are structural facts true of both Apple files). **I ALSO SPOT-CHECKED THE DISCRIMINATION CLAIM MYSELF** rather than take the table on trust: applied M2 by hand, rebuilt, ran the suite — **exactly ONE test red, the vel-0 hazard**, carrying 2 issues (the notes array AND four dangling-note warnings, which incidentally shows decision (2) above working); restored by `cp` from a pre-mutation copy (`git restore` is forbidden in this tree), `shasum -c` OK against pin `723f1dc2`, 39/39 green again. **AND THE MEASUREMENT THAT MATTERS MOST: under that same live M2 bug, my fresh APPLE-generated probe stayed 23/23 GREEN.** So the two-source fixture doctrine is now proven from BOTH directions — the implementer's (Apple green under all 7 mutations) and mine (my own independently-generated Apple fixture green under a real, live bug). An Apple-only gate ships the bug silently. **MY OWN ERROR, disclosed:** my first probe run reported 6 failures that were mine, not the decoder's — Apple emits a leading tempo-only `MTrk` and the decoder deliberately KEEPS it, so writer track *i* is decoder part *i+1*; my expectation table had assumed a 1:1 mapping. The decoder was right and I was wrong. Fixed by adding an explicit structural assertion (3 parts, part 0 tempo-only, the two music parts carrying channels [3] and [9]) so the +1 offset is VERIFIED rather than assumed — otherwise a decoder that silently dropped a real part would still have lined up. I also closed a gap the implementer flagged honestly rather than papered over: the fixture README's expectation table had no row for `apple-type1.mid`, so its numbers were derived from bytes rather than third-party-confirmed; I added the row from my own `MusicSequenceFileLoad` validation run, including the counting convention that trips people (Apple reports 2 tracks because `MusicSequenceGetTrackCount` excludes the tempo track, while the file has 3 chunks and we report 3 parts — both right, counting different things). Deliberately NOT done, and correctly so: no `swift run DAWApp` (zero UI changes, and launching would bind port 17600, which the standing constraint reserves for the user's live app). **One note inherited by k3's brief: program change and poly aftertouch are parsed and framed correctly but DISCARDED, because instrument selection is a mapping decision — if k3 wants program change it must first say what it means by it.** **REBUILD REQUIRED — `dist/DAWPro.app` predates this and thirteen items before it.**
- [x] (m23-k2) **SMF encoder + the asymmetric round-trip.** The inverse of k1: write type 1 (and type 0 where asked) from the same tick-native IR, running status optional but correct if emitted. **GATE — and the shape of it is the point: round-trip through our own decoder is a VACUOUS gate here**, because an encoder and decoder wrong in mirrored ways round-trip perfectly (the m23-d lesson: a check that a broken build also passes is not a check). The load-bearing assertion is ASYMMETRIC — **Apple's `MusicSequenceFileLoad` parses what we wrote and recovers the notes/tempo we meant**. Our own round-trip is kept as a cheap regression net, explicitly labelled as such rather than as proof. Also: byte-level comparison against hand-authored spec bytes for a known-small file. Route: swift-app-engineer. — **DONE 2026-07-26 (swift-app-engineer), ORCH-VERIFIED.** Shipped `Sources/DAWCore/StandardMIDIFileWriter.swift` (`SMFWriteOptions{format, useRunningStatus}`, `SMFEncodeError`, `encode`/`write`) + `Tests/DAWCoreTests/StandardMIDIFileWriterTests.swift`, 55 tests / 1 suite. Still tick-native, still project-free — k2 answers none of k3's mapping questions. **PIN-BEFORE-DELEGATING, APPLIED TO OUTPUT:** I hand-authored three expected-OUTPUT fixtures from the SMF 1.0 spec BEFORE the encoder existed — `encode-minimal.mid` (63 B), `encode-format0-merge.mid` (51 B), `encode-controllers.mid` (57 B) — and validated each through TWO independent readers (Apple's `MusicSequenceFileLoad` AND our own k1 decoder), which agreed on every value, before writing the brief. Twelve policies **P1–P12** are pinned by those bytes and documented in `Tests/DAWCoreTests/Fixtures/SMF/README.md`: tempo to its `sourceTrackIndex` track; note-off spelled `8n` not `9n`-with-0; the `8n` velocity slot carries `releaseVelocity` verbatim (**P2 and P3 are TWO decisions, not one — `9n`-with-0 has no velocity slot to fill**); running status OFF by default; track name at delta 0; EOT at `endTick` even when past the last note; format 0 merges into ONE `MTrk`; **at an equal tick the order is meta → note-off → controller → note-on, holding ACROSS parts in a merge**; merged EOT is the MAXIMUM `endTick`; bend LSB-first; channel pressure two bytes; channel nibble from the event. All three pins matched on the encoder's FIRST run. **THE REAL BUG, and it was the implementer's find:** `SMFNote(tick: Int.max, lengthTicks: 1)` exited **133 (SIGTRAP)** — `tick + lengthTicks` overflowed inside a pure-model type, taking the process down. My brief's twelve refusals each probed their boundary at ±1; none went near where `Int` itself gives out. Fixed with `addingReportingOverflow` + `noteEndTickOverflows`, deliberately an OVERFLOW check and **not a tick ceiling** (absolute ticks above 0x0FFFFFFF are legal SMF so long as no single GAP exceeds it, so a ceiling would refuse files the format permits); `tick 0 / length Int.max` still refuses distinctly as `deltaTimeTooLarge` and a test pins that distinction. A 14th refusal `tooManyTracks` was added at my request — `uint16BE(chunks.count)` masked 65536 to 0, writing a header that lies about its own contents — guarded on the OUTPUT chunk count, so a 70000-part IR written as format 0 has one chunk and an honest `ntrks` of 1. The implementer then audited the whole masking class and left `uint32BE(body.count)` deliberately unguarded (a >4 GiB `MTrk` body fails allocation long before the mask bites), recording the reachability line so k3 does not re-find it and assume it was missed. **DISCRIMINATION, 15 mutations, real counts:** M1 bend bytes swapped → **5** red; M2 note-off velocity hardcoded 0 → 7; M3 channel nibble hardcoded 0 → 7; M4 note-on before note-off at equal tick → 5; M5 note-off spelled `9n` → 13; M6 velocity-0 refusal removed → 1; M7 tempo >2²⁴ refusal removed → 1; M8 running status not cancelled by meta → 1; M9 EOT at last event → 6; M10 merged EOT takes the first part's → 3; M11 zero-length note-off rung → 2; M12 tempo always to chunk 0 → 1; M13 VLQ stops after one byte → 15; M14 overflow guard defeated → 1; M15 `tooManyTracks` guard weakened → 1. No mutation reddened nothing. **FOUR (M1–M4) produced files Apple loads WITHOUT ERROR and reads WRONGLY** — the class an Apple-accepts-it gate cannot see, and precisely why the byte pins and value assertions exist. **ORCH INDEPENDENT VERIFICATION (my runs):** Swift 3681/384 → **3736/385** (+55/+1, exactly the new suite), 72.8 s, only failure the documented `SoundBankHostingTests` T1 flake isolating **7/7 in 0.616 s** at load 24.21. **616 units rebuilt, 0 warnings** — and the warning gate was itself proven non-blind by an injected `var neverMutated` probe that surfaced 6 warning lines before restore. Wire **154/157 UNCHANGED and byte-identical** — zero growth BY DESIGN, the second consecutive item to ship none, because nothing is user-facing until k3/k4. **20 pins verified** (13 k1 fixtures + 3 k2 byte pins + `Commands.swift` + `server.ts` + both k1 sources byte-identical). LAW L9 clean; exactly 16 `.mid` files in the repo, no gate residue. **MY OWN INDEPENDENT EXERCISE:** built an IR the implementer's gate never encodes — division **192** (not 480), channels 5 and 11, off-grid tick 7, a 1-tick note, velocities 12 and 127 at the edges, **release velocity 99**, two tempos, a 7/8 meter, three parts with disagreeing endTicks — encoded it with our writer and checked Apple's reading against a table derived from the IR I BUILT, in a tool that links AudioToolbox and never links DAWCore: **24/24 for format 1 AND format 0**, calibrating to **0/11 and 1/11** against wrong files. I reproduced M1 by hand and got exactly the claimed **5** red tests, and exercised the refusal set directly (`Int.max` refuses instead of trapping; the 24-bit tempo boundary is exact — 0xFFFFFF writes, 0x01000000 refuses). **MY OWN ERROR, and the sharpest lesson of the cycle:** my probe first used pitch bend **16383** as its edge case and under a LIVE M1 it passed **24/24** — 16383 is `0x3FFF`, both 7-bit halves are 127, so swapping them is a NO-OP. I chose the one bend value that cannot detect byte order precisely because it looked like a good boundary. The implementer's own table already carried 8193 with the comment "asymmetric, so a swap shows". Moved to 8193; it now fails 2/24 under M1. **THE APPLE LEG'S BLIND SPOT, stated not glossed:** Apple **REJECTS format 2 outright, OSStatus −10870**. I isolated it by taking my own format-1 output and flipping ONLY the format word byte — the same bytes are accepted as 1 and rejected as 2 — so format 2 is supported and structurally verified (chunk bodies asserted identical to format 1; k1 reads it) but **Apple is NOT the witness for it**. Running status ON vs OFF is byte-affecting and semantics-neutral (86 B vs 91 B, read identically by Apple); my own probe could not test it because P2's `8n` spelling makes note streams alternate 9n/8n so nothing is elidable — which is itself why encoders chasing compression use `9n`-with-0.
- [x] (m23-k3) **Import path: tick-native IR onto the project.** Where the tempo-map decision actually lives, and the reason k1 refuses to make it. Maps a parsed file onto tracks and clips: one DAW track per MIDI track, notes into MIDI clips with the controller lanes populated, and the file's tempo/meter events onto `TempoMap`/`MeterMap` — including the open question of what to do when the imported file's tempo map CONFLICTS with the project's existing one (adopt / ignore / ask), which is a user-facing behaviour decision, not an implementation detail. Wire triple for `project.importMIDI` / `clip.importMIDI` (+ MCP tools + tests), additive at END. **Route: `daw-architect` read on the tempo-map mapping FIRST — this is the read the original m23-k line called for — then swift-app-engineer.** Two things k1/k2 deliberately left for this line, both decisions and not discoveries: (a) **BPM is a LOSSY reading of µs/quarter** — SMF stores microseconds-per-quarter as an integer and `TempoMap` stores `bpm: Double` (666666 µs/qn = 90.000090… BPM), so k3 must state which direction is AUTHORITATIVE before k4's export inherits the question; (b) **program change and poly aftertouch are PARSED BUT DISCARDED** by k1 — k3 says what it means by them (carry them onto the track's instrument? drop them loudly? a warning?) before any line claims MIDI import is complete. **DESIGN READ DONE 2026-07-26, TWO PASSES — `docs/research/m23-k3-midi-import-design.md` (1283 lines). The next cycle IMPLEMENTS; do not re-do the read.** Pass 1 settled the eight hazards + four named decisions and reversed itself once (meter numerator/denominator maps VERBATIM — `6/8` → `(6,8)` — because `beatsPerBar`/`beatUnit` is user-facing at three sites, so a translating importer would ship a bug into k4's export). Pass 2 VERIFIED all 12 load-bearing claims against the tree and MEASURED three that pass 1 had only argued: **two gate legs were vacuous and one rationale was false** — a hoisted reciprocal-multiply does NOT miss integer beats at any division real files use (the single division stands on correctness-by-definition, not fidelity), and the note-length discriminator had to move from a dyadic length (0 of 20,001 on-grid) to a triplet (~20,000). D2's round-trip is now exhaustive: 0 failures over all 2,850,001 µs/qn values in the clamp window, worst error 4.65661e-10 at µ=2,807,175 — orchestrator-reproduced to every digit, as were the reciprocal and tick-23 results. **Pass 2 also surfaced a live pre-existing bug it did NOT cause: `ProjectStore.swift:625-626` computes bar length from `beatsPerBar` alone, ignoring `beatUnit`, so a 6/8 project gets six-quarter-note bars** — filed as its own OPEN entry in ARCHITECTURE.md §"Key future decisions"; whether it lands before k3 is the USER'S CALL, and the shipped default is to land k3 first with the resulting dropped meter change REPORTED, not silent. GATE: a third-party fixture imports with notes landing on the right BEATS (not just the right ticks), tempo/meter honored, conflict policy exercised in both directions, wire round-trip over the control port, suites. — **DONE 2026-07-27 (swift-app-engineer), IMPLEMENTED TO THE TWO-PASS DESIGN.** Shipped four things. **(1) Step 0, the only reopening of a shipped file:** `SMFProgramChangeEvent` + `SMFTrack.programChanges` + `SMFTrack.polyAftertouchEventCount` in `StandardMIDIFile.swift`, both DEFAULTED, and the reader now records `0xC0`'s payload and counts `0xA0` instead of discarding both. **The additivity discriminator the brief named was met exactly: all 94 existing tests (39 reader + 55 writer) pass with ZERO edits to either file** — which is stronger than it looks, since `SMFTrack` is `Equatable` and a stored property changes its synthesized `==`; they stay green only because every construction site takes the new defaults on BOTH sides of every comparison. A program change still does NOT `channels.insert`, so it cannot conjure a phantom part out of a format-0 split (`discardedOnlyChannelYieldsNoPart` pins the other side). **One thing the design left open and the implementation had to decide: the poly-aftertouch count is kept PER CHANNEL in `RawTrack` and summed only for a whole format-1/2 chunk** — a single per-chunk total copied into each format-0 split would over-count by exactly the factor the split exists to create, i.e. the report would lie on the one format the field matters for. Symmetrically, program changes filter by channel on the split; the consequence (a format-0 program change addressed to a channel with no retained event has no part to ride and is absent from the IR) is written into the IR's doc comment as a k4 obligation rather than left to be re-found as a bug. **(2) `Sources/DAWCore/StandardMIDIFileMapper.swift`, the ONE home** — `SMFTickClock` (the one producer of a beat from a tick; its metrical branch routes through a private `metricalLength(lengthTicks:ticksPerQuarter:)` that CANNOT SEE `tick`, so endpoint subtraction is unrepresentable there rather than merely tested against), `MIDIImportOptions`/`MIDIImportContext`/`MIDIImportReport`/`MIDIImportError`, `MIDIImportPlan` with a `fileprivate` init and deliberately NO `Codable`, and `SMFProjectMapper.map` carrying every hazard verdict. Pure, actor-free, store-free. **(3) `Sources/DAWCore/ProjectStore+MIDIFile.swift`** — `importMIDIFile` / `importMIDIIntoClip`, guards first (recording refused uniformly, D1″), extension checked BEFORE existence (so a non-existent `.txt` says "not a MIDI file", the shipped `importSampleLibrary` order), dry run returning before the zero-effect refusal, ONE `performEdit` folding tempo adoption in, and `engine?.tracksDidChange(tracks)` on BOTH paths. **(4) Wire 154 → 156**, `project.importMIDI` + `clip.importMIDI` appended at the END; HEAD's 154 is an exact PREFIX of the new list. **THE ORCHESTRATOR'S PRE-AUTHORED PIN MATCHED IN FULL** — `apple-type1.mid` imports as exactly **2 tracks, not 3** (D4 skips the conductor and REPORTS the skip), notes at beats 0.0/1.0/2.0/3.0 each 0.5 long in both parts, cc11 at beats 0/0.5/1 = 20/60/100 **on each track separately**, tempo segments at beat 0.0 = 120.0 and beat 4.0 = `60_000_000.0/666_666.0` asserted AS THE EXPRESSION (never a decimal literal), **zero meter events → H4c: `fileMeterMap == nil`, `meterChangesAdopted == 0`, nothing synthesized, the project's meter untouched**, `tempoWasClamped` false. Every beat value is exactly representable and is asserted with `==`, not a tolerance. **THE ONE NEW BYTE FIXTURE, and only one:** `hazard-type1-multichannel.mid` (77 B, SHA-256 **eee5e36b653e3d7c0194c2a99c68d9bc72cd09e3671237183e5e9d69be112339**), because its claim is a PARSING claim — that k1 really does hand k3 a format-1 `SMFTrack` with `channels.count == 2` — which no hand-built IR can establish. **Apple's `MusicSequenceFileLoad` confirms the bytes**: one `MusicTrack` carrying `0xB0`/`0xB1` CC 11 at 20 and 100 plus notes on ch 0 and ch 1. The other six hazards are hand-built IR cases in the test file, one hazard each. **TWO VACUOUS LEGS CAUGHT WHILE WRITING THE GATE, both by the design's own measurements:** G2's demonstration half first used tick 30, where the forbidden endpoint-subtraction form happens to AGREE bit-for-bit at t = 480 — moved to tick 120, which diverges (the main invariant half passed either way, so only the "show the bug is real" half was affected, and that is precisely the half that keeps the leg honest); and G1b ships all THREE assertions including the bare-arithmetic positive control `Double(49) * (1.0/49.0) != 1.0`, so the suite itself demonstrates the two forms can diverge instead of asking a future reader to take the measurement on trust. **SUITES: 3736/385 → 3800/388** (+64 tests, +3 suites — exactly `StandardMIDIFileMapperTests` 37, `ProjectStoreMIDIImportTests` 18, `MIDIFileImportCommandTests` 9), full `./scripts/test.sh` GREEN, **622/623 units rebuilt at 0 warnings on a gate proven non-blind by an injected `var vacuityProbe` that surfaced 6 warning lines before restore** (and the FIRST attempt at that gate was itself vacuous: `swift build` piped through `tee` came back as rtk's filtered one-liner `ok (build complete)`, on which `grep -c 'warning:'` honestly reports 0 — re-run under `rtk proxy` for the raw 280-line log). The `SoundBankHostingTests` T1 flake fired on one run and not the next on the identical tree, isolating **7/7 in 0.576 s** — another interleaved non-firing run, consistent with the nondeterministic reading. **SEVEN existing test files needed a one-line edit each** — the `allCommands.count == 154` / `last == "track.reorder"` wire pins, which are supposed to advance — plus `ControlTests`'s every-advertised-command-has-a-route loop (both verbs read a real `.mid` off disk this bare loop does not stage, the `instrument.importSampleLibrary` exclusion rationale verbatim) and `ReferenceCommandTests`'s `dropLast(2)` → `dropLast(4)` tail slice. **DELIBERATELY NOT DONE:** the MCP TypeScript side (delegated separately) — `mcp-server/` is BYTE-UNTOUCHED, and the two tools it still owes are **`project_import_midi`** and **`clip_import_midi`** (157 → 159, no `daw_` prefix, `audit-tools` bijection must stay green). **AND NOT DONE ON PURPOSE:** the 6/8 bar-length bug at `ProjectStore.swift:625-626` is untouched — import behaves as the design says GIVEN that bug, dropping the on-the-file's-barline meter change and REPORTING it with a reason string that names DAW Pro's bar length as the cause rather than reading as a parse failure (`meterChangeIsVerbatimAndItsBarlineDrops` pins both halves of that one decision). **THE DESIGN WAS UNDERSPECIFIED IN FOUR PLACES, all reported rather than smoothed over:** (i) it gave no error case for a `parts` index that names nothing — added `partIndexOutOfRange`, since importing nothing silently is indistinguishable from success; (ii) it gave the mapper a single `atBeat` doing two different jobs (timeline position for `project.importMIDI`, in-clip offset for `clip.importMIDI`) — split into `atBeat` + `contentOffsetBeats` so ALL beat placement stays in the mapper and `notesPastClipEnd` can be computed there; (iii) `ProjectStore`'s controller caps are `@MainActor`-isolated statics that an actor-free mapper cannot read — made `nonisolated` rather than copied, since the mapper is the ONLY enforcement of those caps on a path that builds `Clip` values offline; (iv) it did not say what `clip.importMIDI` does when the selected part has no content — refused with `nothingToImport`, because replacing a clip's notes with nothing is a silent CLEAR and `clip.setNotes` is the command for that. **FOUR DEFECTS FOUND IN REVIEW AFTER THE SUITE WAS ALREADY GREEN, all fixed, and the first one is the lesson:** (1) **`decimate` could return `cap + 1` points.** The stride keeps the grid within budget, but the "always keep the last point" rule needs a slot, and at any n that is an EXACT MULTIPLE of the cap the grid has already spent every one — at n = 32768, cap = 16384 the stride is 2, the grid keeps exactly 16384, and the file's last point sits at odd offset 32767, so the append returned 16385. The mapper is the ONLY enforcement of that cap on the import path (it builds `Clip` values offline and never crosses `setControllerLane`), so nothing downstream would have caught it. **The gate leg asserting the post-condition passed anyway, and that is the point worth keeping: it used `cap * 2 + 3` — the obvious "comfortably over the cap" input — which gives stride 3 and lands at 10925, a THIRD of the budget, and cannot fail however the endpoint rule is written. "Well over the limit" is not a boundary; the multiple-of-the-limit is.** Fixed by replacing the final grid point instead of appending past it, and the suite now sweeps `decimate` exhaustively across caps 2/3/5/16/100 at every n from cap+1 to 4·cap+1, asserting count ≤ cap, both endpoints kept, and strict ordering. (2) **`clip.importMIDI` reported `tracksCreated: 1` having created none** — the mapper has one shape for building content, so a clip import necessarily plans a throwaway `PlannedTrack` whose `Track` is discarded; the count was passed through unzeroed, making the report claim a track appeared in the project. Zeroed, and pinned alongside `store.tracks.count == 1`. (3) **The SMPTE degradation was suppressing METER adoption too, by accident of statement order.** Flipping `resolved` to `.ignore` for an absolute-time file did nothing to the tempo half (`buildTempoMap` already returns `nil` for a SMPTE clock, structurally) and exactly one thing to the meter half: it silently discarded it. §2.2 is explicit that meter survives — meter is a bar-grid concept, not a clock, so "3/4 at tick 0" is a true statement about a SMPTE file in a way that "120 BPM" is not — and §2.5's `(nil, meter?)` case is precisely the mechanism for adopting it with the project's current tempo riding through unchanged. The degradation is now tempo-specific and the report says four things instead of one bit (`resolvedTempoPolicy` "adopt", `tempoSegmentsAdopted` 0, `tempoAdoptionDegradedToIgnore` true, `meterChangesAdopted` N). **The shipped fixture carries no `FF 58`, so it cannot tell the two readings apart** — the new leg is a hand-built IR, because the distinction is invisible on every file in the repo. (4) **`notesPastClipEnd` used `>=` while `controllerPointsPastClipEnd` used `>`** — a clip spans the half-open range `[0, length)`, so content exactly ON the end is equally unreachable either way; both now `>=`, **and the fix is PINNED** (nothing in the suite had put content exactly on the boundary, so no test could tell the two spellings apart and a future reader could have flipped it back silently): a new leg puts a note AND a controller point at exactly the end beat, asserts both counters see them, and carries a longer-clip negative control so the assertion is about the boundary and not about the content existing. Reverting the leg to `>` reddens exactly that one test. **And one hardening, the same class m23-k2 already paid for:** `SMFTickClock.lengthBeats`'s SMPTE branch is the mapper's ONE addition of two ticks and was unguarded. Unreachable from a decoded file (ticks accumulate from ≤4-byte VLQs) but `map(_:options:context:)` is public API over a caller-constructed `StandardMIDIFile`, so a hand-built IR reaches it — now saturating via `addingReportingOverflow`, the posture k2 landed on after `SMFNote(tick: .max, lengthTicks: 1)` SIGTRAPped there. **REBUILD REQUIRED — `dist/DAWPro.app` predates this and every item since m22-g.** **ORCH INDEPENDENT VERIFICATION (my runs, not their report):** Swift **3800/388 PASSED** in 68.6 s (3736/385 → +64/+3). First run showed 3 issues = the KNOWN nondeterministic SoundBankHostingTests T1 flake; the very next run of the same tree was clean at exit 0 — an interleaved clean run is the strongest form of that evidence and it is now recorded a fourth time. **621 units, 0 warnings, and the gate PROVEN NON-BLIND** by injecting a `var neverMutated` probe (6 warning lines) then removing it (0) — note the trap I hit doing it: a second `swift build` piped to `grep -c` counts 0 because the FIRST build already consumed the work, so the warning count must come from ONE build's captured log. Wire **154→156 / 157→159**, HEAD's 152-name list an exact PREFIX of the new one; npm **219/219** including the `audit-tools` bijection (my own hand-rolled bijection check was WRONG — it used a naive `.`→`_` rule when the convention is camelCase→snake_case; the shipped test is the authority). `StandardMIDIFileWriter.swift` byte-identical to its k2 pin, and the k1/k2 TEST files byte-identical too — their 01:21 mtime is a bulk `touch`, not an edit, PROVEN by `StandardMIDIFileWriterTests.swift` still hashing to its k2-era `4137074e…` (a `git diff HEAD` on those files is VACUOUS — they are untracked, so it reports nothing whatever they contain). **MY OWN EXERCISE, authored BEFORE the implementer existed** (scratchpad/m23k3-expected.md, hand-derived from the k1 IR dump): 65/65 direct against the mapper and **22/22 over the control port on staging 17695**, covering dry-run-mutates-nothing, the D4 conductor skip, the full-precision 90.00009000009 (NOT 90.0), H4c's no-meter-map, and ONE undo restoring tempo AND tracks together. **CALIBRATED, so the pass means something:** rounding the BPM reddens 2, importing the contentless conductor reddens 8; the mapper was restored byte-exact (`013995a8…`) after each. Their report nests at `result.report`, which my first probe missed — every PROJECT-STATE check passed while every ENVELOPE check failed, and that split is what identified it as my bug rather than theirs. **POST-CLOSE HARDENING of my own gate, because the calibration above only covered ONE of the two layers my wire probe spans:** the mapper mutations redden PARSE-layer legs, but the two most load-bearing assertions — `DRY RUN MUTATED NOTHING` and `ONE undo also restored the tempo map` — are STORE-layer and were unreachable from them, so at close-out I had zero evidence they could fail at all. Calibrated without touching source by mutating the REQUEST instead: dropping `dryRun: true` turns the first call into a real import and the isolation leg went `PASS` → `FAIL :: 2`, with the unmodified script then returning 22/22 exit 0. **A probe with legs from two layers needs one calibration per layer — "it failed loudly once" only vouches for the layer that failed.**

GATE: **the assertion is THE FILE ON DISK, never the control.** Set 16-bit AIFF in the dialog, export, and confirm the container by MAGIC BYTES with a reader that did not write it (hand-rolled RIFF/`FORM` parsers exist in the m23-m2 scratchpad — reuse them). **A leg asserting "the picker shows AIFF" is the vacuous version and is exactly the m23-m2 double-discards-the-format failure wearing a UI costume.** Every listed param driven over the REAL store; the default path (open dialog, change nothing, export) still byte-identical to today's bounce; sheet captured on staging with NO control label truncated (the m22-g law); suites. **Depends on m23-m1 + m23-m2.** — **DONE 2026-07-27 (ui-design-engineer).** The EXPORT chip and ⌘E now open a named dark-glass sheet; the save panel appears AFTER the choices, so the filename it offers already carries the chosen container's extension. Correct DAW behaviour (Logic/Ableton both do it) but a genuine change from the old chip, which always produced WAV — so "open, change nothing, export is byte-identical to today" is true ON FIRST OPEN and my leg is named that way rather than overclaiming. **CAPTURED AND LOOKED AT** (two states, m22-g law): no label truncates — the depth descriptions WRAP rather than ellipsize. The struck-through feedback corrects it instantly and nothing destructive can happen, so this is a wording nit, not a defect. Implementer-surfaced, both recorded as laws: **`@Observable` + a `didSet` that assigns its own property is a SIGSEGV, not a warning** (the macro rewrites the stored property to computed, so the assignment re-enters its own setter — every test "starts", none finish, exit signal 11, ZERO failing assertions); and **`2>&1 > log` is a SECOND way the 0-warn gate goes blind** (stderr to the terminal, stdout to the log; SwiftPM diagnostics are on stderr, so `grep -c` returns a vacuous 0 at exit 0). Explain floor 90 → 91 (memory's "89" was stale — live floor had been 90 since m23-c2). `DAWEngineTests` took `DAWAppKit` as a TEST-ONLY dep (the `DAWControlTests`-takes-`DAWEngine` precedent; the DAWEngine module stays UI-free). **REBUILD REQUIRED.**

- [x] (m23-m3b) **Single-track MIDI export — the surface.** **SPLIT OUT of m23-m3 2026-07-27 (unrelated deliverable: different store API, different file type, different menu, different gate — bundling it buried a 15-line item behind a net-new dialog).** `store.exportTrackMIDIFile(trackID:path:)` (m23-k4a) has a wire verb and NO surface. The menu has a third option neither verb has — **HIDE it — and that is the one to take**, matching the local "Bounce in Place" precedent (which hides rather than fails) and m23-k4b's panel-narrowing reasoning that a file which cannot work should be unofferable rather than refused after the fact. So: show only for `track.kind == .instrument`. **PREMISE MEASURED BY THE ORCH 2026-07-27, BEFORE DELEGATION — THE PREDICATE IS TOTAL, AND ONE HALF OF THE ELIGIBILITY REASONING ABOVE IS WRONG.** The line reasons that "a file which cannot work should be unofferable", which implies an EMPTY instrument track fails. **IT DOES NOT — it SUCCEEDS**, writing a valid 87-byte SMF (`MThd`) with `notesExported: 0` and `written: true`, because the `nothingToExport` guard counts `tracksExported` (track CHUNKS), not notes. Measured over the wire on staging: empty instrument → file written; audio → refused; bus → refused; **and both refusals wrote NO file** (checked for absence, not just for the error). So `kind == .instrument` is TOTAL: **every track for which the item is shown will export successfully.** DO NOT ADD A GUARD FOR THE EMPTY CASE — a zero-note export of a track you have not played yet is a valid file, and the surprise is filed, not fixed. **THE ONLY REACHABLE FAILURE IS `writeFailed` (an unwritable destination), AND THIS VIEW CANNOT SHOW IT: `TrackListView` swallows every error with `try?` at 7 sites.** ORCH DECISION, not the implementer's: this action ALERTS. The existing `try?` sites are operations that fail as SAFE NO-OPS in known states (a reorder or remove refused mid-recording); an export the user explicitly asked for that fails to write is not a safe no-op, and `FileCommands` already alerts for the sibling whole-project verb. This makes it the first error-surfacing action in `TrackListView` — do NOT refactor the other six. **GATE REACH — ORCH DECISION: a `.contextMenu` CANNOT be opened by `debug.captureUI`, so a predicate-only assertion would NOT discriminate** (`canExportMIDI` can be perfectly correct while the menu ignores it — the BOUNDARY≠DISCRIMINATOR class). The menu's ITEM LIST is therefore extracted into a headless model in **DAWAppKit** (the `ExportDialogModel` precedent — that module exists to make app logic unit-testable), the SwiftUI menu RENDERS FROM IT, and a `debug.*` command echoes it so the WIRING is assertable, not merely the rule. `debug.*` sits off `allCommands` and off MCP, so this costs ZERO wire surface. GATE: item present on an instrument track and ABSENT on audio/bus (both asserted — an always-present item passes a present-only leg); export writes a `.mid` whose bytes parse as an SMF by a reader that did not write it; suites. — **DONE 2026-07-27 (swift-app-engineer).** "Export MIDI…" now sits in the track-header context menu, shown only where `Track.canExportMIDI` holds, opening an `NSSavePanel` (`AudioImportPlan.midiContentTypes`, track name, prompt "Export") onto `store.exportTrackMIDIFile`. **THE ELIGIBILITY RULE HAS ONE DEFINITION AND THREE READERS:** `Track.canExportMIDI` (DAWCore), read by the store's own refusal guard (`ProjectStore+MIDIFile.swift:293`) AND by the menu. **The implementer found a THIRD bare `kind == .instrument` I had not named — `ProjectMIDIExportMapper.swift:498`, the whole-project skip-and-report path — and folded it in**, which is what makes the registry entry TRUE rather than aspirational; pure rename, and the G13 dry-run legs that watch that line stayed green while reddening under their rule mutant. **THE MENU RENDERS FROM THE MODEL, NOT ALONGSIDE IT:** `.contextMenu` is a bare `ForEach` over `AppModel.trackHeaderMenuItems(for:)` with **zero inline `if`s left in the body** (verified by read) — so "the view ignores the model" would have to be a visible edit, not a silent divergence.
