- [x] (m23-m3c) **Stems + mastered-mix export — the surface.** **SPLIT OUT of m23-m3 2026-07-27.** `render.stems` has NO UI AT ALL, which means **m23-m1's headline capability — a mastered mix delivered alongside your stems — is reachable only by an agent today**, and so are `includeMixdown` / `masteredLufsTarget` / m2's depth+container on the stems path. Adds a mode to the m3 dialog (the Logic/Ableton idiom: one export dialog, a "what to export" selector) rather than a second dialog. Route: ui-design-engineer. **NOTE THE PARAM SETS GENUINELY DIFFER and must not be presented as one form:** `excludeTrackIds` exists ONLY on bounce; `includeMasteredMixdown`/`masteredLufsTarget`/`masteredTruePeakCeilingDb`/`includeMixdown` exist ONLY on stems. **SIX PREMISES MEASURED BY THE ORCH 2026-07-27 BEFORE DELEGATION — brief from THESE, not from the sentence above.** (PC6 was measured last and is listed before PC5.)
  **PC1 — THE DESTINATION IS A DIRECTORY, NOT A FILE, so "add a mode to the m3 dialog" changes the TERMINAL STEP, not just the form.** `renderStems(toDirectory:)` writes a SET. The m3 sheet ends in an `NSSavePanel` with a suggested filename, and `ExportDialogModel.suggestedFileName(projectName:)` / `export(store:toPath:)` are both FILE-shaped. Stems needs a directory chooser (`NSOpenPanel`, `canChooseDirectories`). The `debug.exportDialog` seam is likewise file-shaped (`exportToPath`) and needs a directory sibling — additive to a `debug.*` command, so ZERO wire cost.
  **PC2 — THE TRACK-LIST SEMANTICS INVERT BETWEEN MODES.** Bounce takes `excludeTrackIds` (an EXCLUSION); stems takes `trackIds` (an INCLUSION — which tracks GET stems). The same checkbox column would mean opposite things in the two modes, on top of the m23-m3 filed OPEN that the section already speaks three vocabularies. **ORCH DECISION: STEMS MODE SHOWS NO TRACK LIST IN v0.** `trackIds: nil` = all master inputs, which is what a stems export nearly always wants; unlike bounce, where excluding IS the point (the instrumental), you take every stem and simply do not use one. **Instead stems mode shows a READ-ONLY PREVIEW OF THE EXACT FILES THAT WILL BE WRITTEN** — which teaches the eligibility rule below far better than a control that inverts meaning, and mirrors the gate. The wire keeps selection for agents; the UI does not have to in v0.
  **PC3 — STEMS HAVE AN ELIGIBILITY RULE BOUNCE DOES NOT, and it is the SAME predicate m23-m3b left duplicated.** Only MASTER INPUTS get stems: a direct-to-master track OR a bus. Measured on staging — routing "Bass" into bus "Verb" makes it VANISH from the default set (`01 Keys.wav`, `02 Verb.wav` — note the RENUMBERING), and naming it explicitly is REFUSED verbatim: "'Bass' is routed to bus 'Verb' — its signal is part of that bus's stem". **A BUS IS A STEM**; "stems" is not "instrument tracks". The rule currently has THREE uncoordinated readers (`StemPlan.swift:183`, `TrackHeaderMenu.swift:95`, the store's bounce path) and this item needs a FOURTH in the UI — so **CLOSE THE ONE HOME HERE rather than adding a fourth copy** (`Track.isMasterInput`, the `canExportMIDI` shape). **This CLOSES m23-m3b's filed OPEN;** tick it when this lands. This is not scope creep: m3c cannot ship without a UI reader of that rule regardless.
  **PC4 — NO MIXDOWN MODE IS BEING ADDED, and the naming is a trap.** `includeMixdown` writes "00 Mixdown.wav" INSIDE the stems directory — it is a stems PARAM, not the `render.mixdown` VERB (the frozen raw/fast/no-report sibling). The m3 line's "render.stems and render.mixdown have ZERO UI" will otherwise read as a brief for two modes. Measured file set with both siblings on: `00 Mixdown.wav`, `00 Mastered Mix.wav`, `01 Keys.wav`, `02 Verb.wav`.
  **PC6 — THE PREVIEW HAS NO SOURCE OF TRUTH TO READ YET, and this is the ONE piece of net-new DAWCore this item needs.** The stem names DO have a home — `StemPlan.descriptors(tracks:including:format:)` is public, pure and headless, and its `fileName` already does numbering, illegal-character sanitizing and duplicate-name disambiguation. **The preview must CALL it, never mirror it.** But the two SIBLINGS do not: `"00 Mixdown"` and `"00 Mastered Mix"` are STRING LITERALS inside `renderStems` (`ProjectStore+Render.swift:317` and `:350`), so a preview that shows them would be the THIRD and FOURTH copy. **Give them a home and have BOTH write sites read it** (`StemPlan.mixdownBaseName` / `.masteredMixdownBaseName`), then add ONE producer of the ordered set — `StemPlan.fileSet(tracks:including:includeMixdown:includeMasteredMixdown:format:) throws -> [String]` — that the preview renders and a test asserts against what `renderStems` ACTUALLY WROTE. That equality test is the whole point: it is what stops the preview and the disk drifting.
  **PC5 — depth + container are ALREADY SHARED**: `renderStems` takes `bitDepth`/`container` and resolves through the SAME `DeliveryFormat.resolve` FIRST, before any file lands (an invalid format rejects the whole set rather than part-way). So the m3 sheet's existing quality controls apply to stems unchanged — do not build a second pair.
 GATE: as m3, plus **the EXACT FILENAME SET on disk asserted by a reader that did not write it — not a file COUNT.** A count-only leg passes a wrong-numbering bug; the fixture MUST include a bus-routed track so the eligibility filter and the RENUMBERING are exercised rather than assumed (route one in, expect `02 <bus>.wav` and NOT `03`). Assert the two optional siblings appear only when asked, and that a stems export honours depth/container by MAGIC BYTES like m3. **Depends on m23-m3.** — **DONE 2026-07-27 (ui-design-engineer).** The EXPORT sheet gained a SONG|STEMS selector; stems mode hides the track list, shows the two sibling toggles (mastered target nested under its toggle) and a LIVE READ-ONLY PREVIEW of the exact filenames, and ends in an `NSOpenPanel` directory chooser. The m3 depth/container controls are shared unchanged (PC5 held). **m23-m1's mastered-mix-beside-stems is reachable without an agent at last.**
  **THE ONE HOME CLOSED, BOTH HALVES.** `Track.isMasterInput` now backs `StemPlan.swift:183` and `TrackHeaderMenu.swift:95` — **this CLOSES the duplication OPEN m23-m3b's implementer filed.** The two `PlaybackGraph` sites stayed out deliberately (766 carries an extra `sends.isEmpty` term; 1297 is a differently-shaped label) — they must stay free to diverge. **PC3 measured as TWO readers, not three: `bounceTrackInPlace` plans through `StemPlan.descriptors`, so it was never a third copy.** And the two sibling names got a home (`StemPlan.mixdownBaseName` / `.masteredMixdownBaseName`) read by BOTH write sites, under one producer `StemPlan.fileSet(...)` that the preview calls.
  **ORCH INDEPENDENT VERIFICATION (my runs, not their report).** Swift **4003/407** (3979/405 → +24/+2). **Exit is NOT 0: 5 issues across 2 tests, and I attributed every one before accepting them.** 3 are `SoundBankHostingTests` **T1**, a KNOWN pre-existing flake (AU preparation timed out after 10 s). 2 are `ReferenceLaneTransparencyTests:390/425`. **MY FIRST ATTRIBUTION WAS WRONG AND MEASUREMENT CORRECTED IT.** I proved structurally that the test is rate-fragile — `LimiterEffect.swift:137` derives `delaySamples` from `lookaheadSeconds * sampleRate` while the test HARDCODES 240, so at the observed 24 kHz graph rate 5 ms = 120 and `|120/24000 − 240/24000| = 0.005`, exactly the two observed values — and then called it "deterministic given the audio device". **It is not.** Measured after: it **PASSES in isolation on this tree**, **PASSES in isolation in a detached worktree at `8b163c9`** (test file byte-identical, `shasum` checked), and **PASSED in the full suite at the m23-m3b baseline hours earlier**. So it is INTERMITTENT and suite-interaction-driven — something earlier in the suite leaves the shared graph at a rate the hardcoded 240 does not describe. **Not caused by m3c** (which touches no DAWEngine source), and now filed with its mechanism known: rate-fragile BY CONSTRUCTION plus rate-UNSTABLE under the full suite, and it takes both to fail. **The arithmetic explaining the observed numbers was necessary and NOT sufficient — a counterfactual claim needed a counterfactual measurement.** (The implementer reported "exactly 2 issues"; T1 is nondeterministic, so both counts are honest for their own run.) npm untouched, `server.ts` sha UNCHANGED. **Wire byte-identical (`shasum -c`): `mcp-server/src/server.ts`, `Tests/DAWControlTests/RenderCommandTests.swift` — 158 commands / 161 MCP tools, `debug.exportDialog` off both.**
  **MY OWN GATE: 23/23 over staging 17695**, every load-bearing leg reading the DIRECTORY with `readdirSync` — a reader that did not write those bytes and does not import `StemPlan`. Bus-routed fixture throughout, so eligibility and RENUMBERING are exercised, not assumed: `01 Keys.wav` + `02 Verb.wav`, Bass ABSENT, no `03`. **MY MUTANT TESTED THE ONE-HOME CLAIM ITSELF rather than trying to break it: I RENAMED the shared `masteredMixdownBaseName` constant and PREDICTED "B2, B5, C1 red; A5 and B3 GREEN" — exactly what happened.** The plan-vs-disk EQUALITY survived a rename that broke every absolute-name expectation, which is positive proof the preview and the writer read the same source. Restored byte-exact (`shasum -c` OK), clean re-run 23/23.
  **THREE FINDINGS FROM MY OWN GATE'S FAILURES, all mine, all worth keeping.** (i) **The stems TOGGLES and the MODE are sticky within a session too** — m23-m3's stickiness finding extends past the format. My first run left the app in stems/both-siblings and three legs failed on a perfectly correct build; the implementer's format-only premise guard does not catch it, so mine now checks mode + both toggles. (ii) **REMOVING A BUS RE-HOMES ITS CHILDREN TO MASTER** — the obvious "empty partition" fixture (route into a bus, delete the bus) does NOT produce one; only a trackless project does. (iii) **`open: true` is REQUIRED before any export leg**: `prepare(tracks:)` runs when the sheet opens, so without it the snapshot is empty and every plan is legitimately `[]` — a gate that skipped it would read an empty directory and call it a defect.
  **Implementer additions I accepted:** an empty master-input partition plans NOTHING (not even the `00 …` siblings, since `renderStems` throws before creating the directory) — my D1/D2 confirm it; and the taller stems card overflowed the 640 pt `WindowFloor`, so `ExportSheetLayout` + a new DESIGN-LANGUAGE law landed ("a named in-window sheet is bounded by the WINDOW, and hugs before it scrolls"). **Honest limit they stated and I am not softening: at the floor the file list sits below the fold, and `debug.captureUI` cannot scroll — scrollability is verified BY CONSTRUCTION plus an arithmetic test, not by a capture.** Equality of plan and disk **holds on the success path only** — the mastered pass runs last and can throw with stems already written.
  **NEW OPEN they found and did not fix (right call): `renderStems` does not tick `renderCompletedCount`,** so completing a real stems export does not advance the onboarding tour's export step. Unreachable before this item; reachable now. Ticking it is a live behaviour change on a wire command (an agent's `render.stems` would advance a human's tour), so it needs its own item and its own gate leg.

- [ ] (m23-r) **Per-insert spectrum tap — promoting the filed m22-b-2 now that it has user demand.** User report: "EQ on any of the tracks does not show the dynamic frequency, only the control/adjust curve, while EQ on the master shows the frequency waves along with the curve." **Working as designed, not a bug** — m22-b's v1 scope was deliberately "curve editor for ALL EQ instances + spectrum overlay on the MASTER strip's EQ only," because the master already had a 24-band analysis running for free while every other insert would need a NEW render-thread tap; the m22-b pixel review even captured "track curve honestly spectrum-free" as the expected state. The seam is already designed and named: `InsertAnalysisTapping` in `docs/research/design-m22b-eq-curve-editor.md` §4.3, with `ChainEffectUnit.processActive` (:175) identified as the host and the cost argument recorded at §4.3/C-1. This item implements it: per-insert analysis tap feeding the SAME `SpectrumBandFold` geometry the master path uses (so track and master curves stay point-comparable), opt-in per open editor so idle inserts pay nothing, and the spectrum overlay enabled for every EQ instance. Follows the M21 precedent — a NOT-scheduled disposition with user demand gets built. Route: daw-architect to confirm §4.3 still holds against the current chain walk → audio-dsp-engineer (tap) → ui-design-engineer (overlay). GATE: measured render-thread cost with N inserts open vs closed (stated budget, no allocation on the audio thread), track spectrum vs master spectrum point-comparable on the same program, bit-exactness of the audio path unchanged under the standing m22-a/b null pin (fingerprint 04e86aec9a73c505), staging capture of a track EQ with a live spectrum, suites.
  **FIVE PREMISE CORRECTIONS, MEASURED 2026-07-27 by the orch pass before delegating:**
  **PC1 — THE LINE'S OWN CENTRAL CLAIM IS FALSE, and following it literally would FAIL the line's own gate.** It says the tap should feed "the SAME `SpectrumBandFold` geometry the master path uses (so track and master curves stay point-comparable)". **The master path does not use `SpectrumBandFold` at all** — `grep` finds zero references to it in `MasterMixAnalyzer.swift`. `SpectrumBandFold` is used by `ReferenceAnalyzer` and `AudioContentAnalyzer` only. The master EQ overlay is fed by **`MasterMixAnalyzer`'s OWN 24 geometric bands, 40 Hz→16 kHz** (`bandCount = MasterAnalysisSnapshot.bandCount = 24`, `Model.swift:2004`; floor −80 dB). **The correct target is the design doc's, not the roadmap's: "reuse the 24-band shape" = produce a `MasterAnalysisSnapshot`.** Building on `SpectrumBandFold` would make the track curve NOT point-comparable with the master — the exact failure the sentence was trying to prevent.
  **PC2 — point-comparability can be made STRUCTURAL, not coincidental.** `MasterMixAnalyzer.bandEdges` (`:116`, `public static let`, 25 edges derived from `bandCount`) is the **single producer**: the folding loop reads it at `:248` and `bandBinStart`/`bandBinCount` (`:157-158`) are derived caches, not an independent recomputation. So an insert analyzer that derives its edges from that same symbol inherits comparability by construction — the ONE-home shape. **It must not become a third home.** Caveat from §4.3 that is easy to miss: "reuse the shape" means reuse the **type and band edges**, with **fixed inline storage** — `MasterMixAnalyzer.snapshot()` allocates a 24-element array and is main-actor-tier, so it may NOT be called render-side.
  **PC3 — the UI leg is nearly FREE; the line bills it as a third agent's work.** `EQCurveEditor` is **already parameterized**: `showsSpectrum: Bool` (`:45`) and `spectrum: () -> MasterAnalysisSnapshot` (`:49`), with the master passing `appModel.vibeSeed ?? store.masterAnalysis()` and track strips passing `showsSpectrum: false` ("Track-strip EQ editors draw NO spectrum in v1 — display honesty", `:44`). Enabling a track spectrum is flipping that flag and supplying a different closure. The help string at `:122` ("the live **master-mix** spectrum") is hardcoded master-specific and must change with it.
  **PC4 — FOUR STALE ANCHORS in the cited design doc** (written 2026-07-20; the tree has moved): `ChainEffectUnit.processActive` is at **:180**, not :175. `EffectChainProcessor.process` is at **:339 and :348** (two overloads), not :334-343. The AudioEngine master tap is at **~:3005-3083** (`installMeterTap`), not :2470-2515. `masterAnalysis()` is at **:3087**, not :2522-2524. Hand these over rather than letting a design pass spend itself on archaeology.
  **PC5 — §4.3's cost argument still HOLDS and is confirmed against current code.** The master path genuinely rides AVFoundation's serial tap queue off the render thread (`installTap` at `AudioEngine.swift:3032`) and genuinely publishes via `Task { @MainActor }` (`:3071`) — both forbidden render-side, exactly as the doc argued. So the deferral was honest and the hard part of this item is real: a preallocated lock-free publish plus an arming policy, not a UI change. **(CORRECTED at design time — see PC6: the *publish* half of this dissolves; only the arming policy survives as the hard part.)**
  **PC6 — THIS LINE'S OWN GATE CITES A VACUOUS PIN, found by the design pass and CONFIRMED by the orch pass.** The gate says "bit-exactness of the audio path unchanged under the standing m22-a/b null pin (fingerprint `04e86aec9a73c505`)". **That pin structurally cannot see this item.** `Tests/DAWEngineTests/EQv2Tests.swift:229-241` constructs `EQEffect(params:)` directly and compares it against an in-test `LegacyEQReference`; `grep -c "ChainEffectUnit\|EffectChainProcessor"` in that file returns **0**. It never touches the chain walk this item modifies. **The real chain-walk pin is `nullEraChainRenderByteIdentical`** (`Tests/DAWEngineTests/ChainClickPolishTests.swift:524`, SHA `0a6fc1c5d68c025bbde78b7fb4c9c3062f606494a042936316ff323d743bcd45`), and the real discriminator is **armed-vs-disarmed byte identity at processor level** — see r2's gate. Keeping the old citation would have shipped a gate leg that passes no matter what the tap does.
  **DESIGN DONE 2026-07-27 — `docs/research/design-m23r-per-insert-spectrum-tap.md` (daw-architect; supersedes `design-m22b-eq-curve-editor.md` §4.3). SPLIT into r1 → r2 → r3 → r4; this umbrella stays for context and is ticked only when all four are.** **THE HEADLINE CORRECTION: the FFT does not have to run on the render thread, and it must not.** §4.3 assumed "the tap point is on the render thread" implied "the analysis is on the render thread" — invalid. The render-side tap reduces to **two bounded `memcpy`s into a preallocated SPSC ring** (release/acquire `daw_atomic_u64`, already shipped at `CAtomics.h:31-35`); the FFT, band fold, ballistics and snapshot run on the **consumer** inside an **UNMODIFIED `MasterMixAnalyzer`**. That dissolves §4.3's problems (a) and (b) outright, needs no new C shim, and satisfies PC2 at the strongest level available — **not shared constants but a shared implementation**, so nothing is extracted and there is no null-pin risk. Also corrected: **the hook belongs in the WALK LOOP BODY (after `EffectChainProcessor.swift:378`), NOT in `processActive`** — orch-confirmed that `if fadeActive { renderCrossfade } else if !bypassedNow { processActive }` means a steadily-bypassed unit calls NEITHER, and `renderCrossfade` calls `processActive` at `:220` BEFORE its equal-power mix at `:224-236`, so a tap in `processActive` misses two of three paths. That was a semantic error in §4.3, not a stale line number. Tap is **POST-insert** (matches the master's post-chain tap; a PRE tap would reproduce the very "my EQ does nothing on screen" complaint that opened this item). Budget **N = 8, structurally enforced by REFUSING the 9th, never evicting**.
- [ ] (m23-r1) **`InsertSpectrumTap` — the SPSC ring + the consumer, standalone.** **SPLIT from m23-r 2026-07-27.** New `Sources/DAWEngine/Analysis/InsertSpectrumTap.swift` + `Tests/DAWEngineTests/InsertSpectrumTapTests.swift`. No chain integration, no UI, no wire — the class is constructed directly by tests and fed `AudioBufferList`s by hand, so SPSC correctness is proven in isolation BEFORE anything touches the walk or the pins. Ring 16_384 frames (341 ms @ 48 k = 2× the 8192 max quantum), drain ≤ 4_096, ~230 KB per armed insert and **0 when disarmed**. Route: audio-dsp-engineer. GATE (both directions): irregular chunk sizes (37/512/1024/3000) drain to bands **bit-identical** to a fresh `MasterMixAnalyzer` fed the same stream in one shot (positive) **and that same assertion FAILS under one dropped 1024-frame block** (discriminator); `droppedFrames == 0` at normal cadence and increments-then-recovers under a forced stall; the tear defence discards-and-counts a stomped staged block; a 1-channel write equals duplicated stereo; `malloc_zone_statistics` delta == 0 across 10 000 quanta **paired with an index-advance assertion** so a tap that allocated nothing because it did nothing cannot pass. **Pin `bands` bit-exactly but NOT `correlation`/`width`/`balance` — those accumulate per-chunk Float partials into Double sums and are legitimately chunk-dependent in the last ulp; pinning them manufactures a fourth flake.**
- [ ] (m23-r2) **Chain integration + arm plumbing — the null-pin cycle.** **SPLIT from m23-r 2026-07-27.** The hook after `EffectChainProcessor.swift:378`, `tapSlot` on `ChainEffectUnit` (`daw_atomic_ptr` + ≥1 s retire bin, the `publish` recipe verbatim), `armedEffectIDs` re-applied inside `sync` every pass, and the arm-intent ONE HOME in `AudioEngine` **re-applied after every graph rebuild** (`:424`/`:951` — the easy-to-miss third re-application point; without it a mid-session rebuild leaves the editor open and the spectrum permanently dead, the exact m3c failure class). Follows the m22-e gain-reduction path verb for verb incl. the `EngineProtocol.swift:954` extension-defaults idiom so a fake engine is honestly unarmed rather than fabricating floors. **Arming must never republish the chain** (identity is `(id, kind)` only) so opening/closing the editor cannot reset DSP state or click. Route: audio-dsp-engineer. GATE: **the REAL pin `ChainClickPolishTests.swift:524` (SHA `0a6fc1c5…`) stays green — NOT the vacuous `04e86aec9a73c505` (PC6)** — plus the actual discriminator, **armed-vs-disarmed byte identity at processor level, asserted in BOTH directions** — bit-identical output, AND liveness proven by **TWO SEPARATE assertions that do not substitute for each other**: (i) the armed run's ring **write index advanced**, and (ii) the drained bands are **non-floor**. They fail differently and neither implies the other — a ring that advances while the drain path is broken yields floor bands and passes (i) alone; a tap reading a stale prior fill yields non-floor bands and passes (ii) alone. Both are required, or "identical because the tap never ran" slips through; a **pre/post pin** where the tapped 3 kHz band differs from a dry-fed reference by ≈ the cut depth (**a PRE tap fails this leg**); one leg per strip type (instrument via `InstrumentSourceNode`, audio/bus + master via `ChainHostAU`) or a whole strip class is silently spectrum-free; bypass honesty; the full arm lifecycle incl. kind change, `invalidateEffect`, rate change and **graph rebuild with the arm held**; the 9th arm refused with `tapSlot` nil; the three budget legs printed `[measured]`. **Every leg must arm → render ≥100 ms → poll: a freshly armed tap legitimately reads `.floor` (needs ≥2048 frames + a drain), so a leg that arms and immediately reads is measuring the premise, not the feature.**
- [ ] (m23-r3) **UI enablement — a HALF cycle, and must not be padded into a full one.** **SPLIT from m23-r 2026-07-27; PC3 confirmed by the design pass.** Flip `showsSpectrum` at `EffectEditorOverlay.swift:143`, make the `EQCurveEditor.swift:49` closure target-aware, add a `.task(id: target)` that arms on entry and releases on cancellation, and **fork the hardcoded master-specific help string at `:122`** — the track copy must say the fill is the spectrum **after** this EQ, which is the POST decision made visible. Plus `debug.insertSpectrumSeed` beside `debug.vibeSeed` (app-tier, off `allCommands`/MCP) so the capture is deterministic. Route: ui-design-engineer. GATE: staging capture of a track EQ card with a live spectrum AND the master card unchanged from m22-b; with the tap disarmed the track card draws the **floor silhouette, not a fabricated one**; arm/disarm proven **through the seam, not "the pixels changed"**; and the close-out must **explicitly supersede the m22-b pixel review's "track curve honestly spectrum-free" capture** — it was recorded as expected state and a future reviewer must not read it as a pin.
- [ ] (m23-r4) **`fx.spectrum` + MCP tool — the constitution's every-capability rule.** **SPLIT from m23-r 2026-07-27.** Strictly additive; never touches `fx.setParam`/`fx.describe`. `fx.spectrum {trackId?, effectId, arm?}` → `{bands[24], levelDb, peakDb, centroidHz, flux, armed}`. Deliberately **NOT** on the always-on `project.snapshot` payload (the `MasterScopeFrame` precedent — raw data stays off the always-on wire). The **TTL lease (3 s, lazily swept)** lives here and ONLY here, above the engine's refcount-free arm; the N=8 cap is its backstop so a leaked lease can never grow unbounded. Route: mcp-integration-engineer. GATE: arm → play ≥200 ms → non-floor bands whose **peak band matches an injected tone's band** (a real discriminator, not "24 numbers came back"); a silent track returns the floor exactly; the 9th arm refused with a **teaching error naming the cap**; lease expiry proven; unknown ids refused without partial mutation; MCP schema rejects an unknown key at the boundary; **wire count moves by exactly the additive delta and is pinned**. **m23-o runs after r3 and does NOT need r4.**
- [ ] (m23-s) **Insert-row labels truncate on exactly the rows that carry GR mini-bars.** ORCH FINDING during m23-a verification, and **attributed away from m23-a**: at the 132 pt strip width an insert row's name and m22-e's five-cell gain-reduction bar contend for the same line, so `Compressor` renders `Co...` and `Limiter` renders `Lim...`, while bar-less rows (`Saturator`, `Chorus`, `Delay`, `Reverb`) render full. Verified NOT caused by m23-a's new scroller: identical in the hugging branch with no scrollbar present, and identical in the pre-fix `before/` capture. So this is **pre-existing m22-e** — but it violates the law codified at the m22-g close (`docs/DESIGN-LANGUAGE.md`: shared control components pin their own width; **a control label never truncates** and a digital readout never scales), which did not exist when m22-e shipped. `Co...` is also genuinely ambiguous against `Chorus`. Options, cheapest first: drop the GR bar to its own line on the rows that have one (the `KEY` sub-row precedent is right there); shorten the bar; or give the row a two-line anatomy. Pure-view, wire/MCP delta ZERO. Route: ui-design-engineer. GATE: every built-in effect kind captured in a strip at 132 pt with its GR bar active, no label truncated, plus the m22-e GR behaviour itself unchanged (unpaused `.periodic` law).
- [ ] (m23-t) **The piano-roll header's cyan BAR readout says BAR 1 when the transport is elsewhere.** Filed 2026-07-26 by the m23-c1 implementer, verified by the orch pass. `PianoRollView.swift:365` renders `Text("BAR \(targetBarNumber)")` in `DAWTheme.playback` under the in-source comment `// cyan = position readout`, and `:353` documents the v1 fallback as "acts on the bar under the playhead, or **the first bar when the transport is elsewhere**". So with the transport 4 bars outside the edited clip the header shows a cyan readout, labelled in the source as a POSITION readout, reading `BAR 1` — captured in m23-c1's own S7 frame. This is the SAME honesty law m23-c1 just amended, on a surface m23-c1 deliberately did not touch. It is not dishonest about what the +/− buttons will DO (they really do act on bar 1), so the likely fix is a RELABEL — it is an OPERAND readout, not a position one — rather than a behavior change; cyan may also be wrong there, since Rule 3 reserves the accent for earned active state and the operand is not the transport. Route: ui-design-engineer. Cheap. GATE: capture with the transport off-clip showing the readout no longer claims to be a position; suites.
- [ ] (m23-u) **Live MIDI thru is cut under a still-held note on a never-played track — the same render hole m23-d just fixed for audition.** FILED 2026-07-26 by the m23-d fix pass (found while fixing audition; deliberately NOT fixed there, because closing it means editing the ID-pairing branches m16-b3 §4.3 C11 governs and nothing in m23-d's gate covers them). `InstrumentSourceNode` step 7's silence fast-path returns before step 9's `instrument.render(...)`, so an instrument holding an open voice is skipped on every event-free quantum. m23-d's `liveTailRemaining` countdown MITIGATES thru (a held thru note now sounds for `liveTailSeconds` = 8 s instead of one quantum) but does not CLOSE it: audition tracks `openAuditionCount` and re-arms the tail while anything is held; thru tracks no equivalent, so the tail expires under a note the user is still physically holding down. **Masked, which is why m3-vii shipped without a report:** `schedule == nil` holds only on a node where a schedule was NEVER published, and after any playback one stays published — so it needs a cold app, a track that has never played, and a note held longer than 8 s. Fix shape: an `openLiveCount` incremented on a note-on that finds `pitchToLiveID[p] == 0`, decremented on a matched off, zeroed everywhere `pitchToLiveID` is cleared (steps 1 and 1b), and added to 6d's re-arm condition. The §4.3 C11 rule that controller events (kind ≥ 2) must never touch the pitch map applies to the counter too. `LiveThruRenderTests`'s "the tail is BOUNDED" assertion documents the current behavior in place and is where the fix should turn red first. Route: `daw-architect` read on the C11 interaction → `audio-dsp-engineer`. GATE: a held thru note sustains past `liveTailSeconds` against a REAL instrument asserting AUDIO (the `AuditionSustainTests` harness is the model — `EventCaptureInstrument` writes no audio and structurally cannot see this class), an idle node still silent from its first quantum, no allocation on the render thread, suites.
- [ ] (m23-v) **A brand-new instrument track still LOOKS inert — the path into the note editor works but is not DISCOVERABLE.** FILED 2026-07-26 by the m23-e orch pass, carrying the half of m23-e that was deliberately NOT built. m23-e shipped the empty-lane double-click, which answers the mechanical half of the user's report ("adding a track leaves no affordance to start writing notes") — after adding a track you can now double-click the lane and land in a writable grid. What it does NOT answer is the word AFFORDANCE: a double-click is invisible, so a new user still sees an empty lane that promises nothing. **The roadmap's original phrasing — a new-track ACTION that also creates a clip — was considered and REJECTED, and the reasoning should survive:** `store.addTrack` is called from three UI sites plus the wire's `track.add`, so auto-creating a clip inside it either changes `track.add`'s semantics (forbidden — additive-only, never change a live command) or forks UI-add from wire-add, breaking the one-command-surface rule that makes this app agent-controllable. It would also entangle the clip's undo step with the track's. Build the DISCOVERABILITY instead, which is UI-only and touches no shared seam: the hover ghost line already renders over empty lane space (`handlePointerHover` → `ghostBeat`), so the cheap honest version is an empty-instrument-lane hint (e.g. a dim "double-click to add a clip" that appears on hover or while the track has no clips) plus the `.explainable` copy. Route: `ui-design-engineer`. GATE: a new instrument track shows the hint with zero clips and stops showing it once it has one; the hint never appears on audio/bus lanes (where the double-click legitimately refuses); no wire growth; suites.
- [ ] (m23-w) **`clip.removeMany {ids:[uuid]}` + `clip.moveMany {ids:[uuid], byBeats:Double}`, plus MCP `clip_remove_many` + `clip_move_many` — the wire half of m23-g1's group delete AND m23-g2's group drag.** SCOPE WIDENED 2026-07-26 by the m23-g2 orch pass: g2 shipped `ProjectStore.moveClips(ids:byBeats:)` under the same zero-wire-growth preference, and its own doc comment at `ProjectStore.swift:3751` already says it is "filed as roadmap item m23-w together with group delete's" — the code asserted this line's scope before this line did. Do both verbs in one pass; they share the validate-then-one-`performEdit` shape and the round-trip test file. FILED 2026-07-26 by the m23-g1 orch pass, as a decision the user can reverse rather than an oversight. m23-g1 shipped `ProjectStore.removeClips(ids:)` reachable only from the UI and a debug-tier seam, because the brief PREFERRED zero wire growth and the implementer honored it — but CLAUDE.md's constitution says every user-facing capability ships a control command AND an MCP tool AND a test, and "delete several clips as one undoable edit" is now a real capability an agent cannot reach. Today an agent must issue N `clip.remove` calls, which is N undo steps — precisely the behaviour m23-g1 exists to eliminate, so the agent-facing surface is strictly worse than the human-facing one. Strictly ADDITIVE (never rename/alter `clip.remove`). The store verb, its all-or-nothing validation and its `requireNotCompMember` enforcement already exist and are tested (9 tests in `RemoveClipsTests`), so this is wire plumbing plus a round-trip test, not new domain logic. Route: `mcp-integration-engineer`. GATE: round-trip over the control port removing 3 clips across 2 tracks in exactly ONE `edit.history` step; a take-group member refused with the same error the single-clip path gives; unknown id refused without partial mutation (all-or-nothing); MCP tool registered and its arg schema rejecting an unknown key at the boundary; suites + npm. GATE for the `moveMany` half — the response must surface `ClipsMoveResult`'s `effectiveDeltaBeats` / `clamped` / `trimmedClipIDs` / `removedClipIDs` rather than a bare ok, because a group move can legitimately deliver LESS than asked (whole-group floor clamp) and an agent that cannot see that will compound the error on its next call: move 3 clips by −8 beats when the leftmost sits at beat 2 and assert `effectiveDeltaBeats == -2`, `clamped == true`, and all three spacings intact. **Carry m23-g2's disclosed hole forward, do not re-lose it: `moveClips` sorts its movers, but that sort is NOT covered — the implementer's own mutant that deleted it passed 35/35, because every fixture used non-mutually-overlapping movers.** The uncovered case is two MOVING clips that overlap each other's destinations on the same track; add a test that fails without the sort before adding wire access, since the wire makes it reachable by an agent that will not hand-check the result.
- [ ] (m23-x) **Arrow-key nudge for arrange clips (single AND group).** **FILED 2026-07-26 by the m23-g2 orch pass, which SPLIT it out of g2's title.** Grep-verified net-new: the app has ZERO arrow-key handling anywhere (`leftArrow`/`rightArrow`/`upArrow`/`downArrow`/`keyboardShortcut(.left)` → no hits in `Sources/DAWApp` or `Sources/DAWAppKit`), so this does not exist even for a single clip — it was never a group-awareness gap. It is also absent from m23-g's gate entirely; it lived only in g2's old title. Runs AFTER g2 because it is a thin keyboard layer over the SAME group-move verb g2 must build (`moveClips(ids:byBeats:)`), and building it first would mean building that verb twice. **Design questions to answer explicitly rather than assume: (1) step size** — the current snap grid, or a fixed musical unit, with a modifier for fine (1/32, `minClipLengthBeats`) vs coarse (bar)? **(2) undo coalescing** — holding an arrow must NOT produce 40 undo steps; `performEdit` takes a `key:` for exactly this, but note g1's finding that the existing keys are PER-CLIP (`clip.move:<uuid>`), so a group nudge needs a selection-stable key. **(3) guards** — mirror `handleArrangeDeleteKey()` exactly (workspace mode, `isModalPresented`, text-editing focus, empty selection); arrows are far more likely than DELETE to be pressed while a text field is focused, so the text guard is load-bearing, not defensive. **(4) does the beat-0 clamp match g2's** (whole-group, offsets preserved)? It must, or the two paths disagree. Route: swift-app-engineer. GATE: GUARD-TESTING LAW — drive the guards through the handler they live on, and prove each refusal with a CONTROLLED PAIR (guard inactive → nudges; guard active → refuses), or a dead handler passes; a 3-clip nudge is ONE `edit.history` step; N repeated nudges coalesce to the documented number of steps (assert the DEPTH, per g1's vacuity finding — "the clips moved" cannot discriminate); relative offsets survive a nudge into the beat-0 clamp; suites.
- [ ] (m23-y) **Track multi-select in the arrange.** SPLIT OUT of m23-g3 2026-07-26 by the orch pass: it had no gate leg of its own there, and its semantics question was left open with a `Route:` line attached — which is how an implementer ends up inventing a policy. **THE SEMANTICS QUESTION IS ANSWERED HERE, deliberately, so it is a decision the user can overrule rather than one an agent makes silently. DEFAULT (build this): selecting a track SELECTS ITS CLIPS.** Under that reading `ArrangeSelection` needs NO new state, group delete needs NO new rule, and a mixed track+clip selection is just a bigger `ids` set — it composes with everything m23-g1 and m23-g2 already built. **ALTERNATIVE (only on the user's word): a track is selected AS A DISTINCT OBJECT**, which requires a second selection domain alongside clips AND forces group-delete to decide whether it removes clips or whole tracks — a materially larger item, and one that would put a destructive verb behind a new ambiguity. `ArrangeSelection.swift:29-31` records that the type takes no position on this, so whichever reading ships must be written into that comment. Prerequisite: none beyond g3's set-mutator. GATE: clicking a track header selects every clip on that track; shift-clicking a second header adds its clips without dropping the first's; group DELETE over a mixed selection removes exactly the union in ONE `edit.history` step (the m23-g1 atomicity claim, re-proven on the mixed shape); a track with zero clips selects cleanly and deletes nothing; suites. Route: `swift-app-engineer` (domain/selection, not pixels).
- [ ] (m23-q) **DESIGN SPIKE: note-level vocal pitch correction (Melodyne / Auto-Tune class) — GO/NO-GO, not an implementation item.** The user's largest ask: split a vocal into note-level segments by detected melody, then edit pitch/timing per segment, plus vocal alignment and professional correction. This is a multi-week subsystem (monophonic pitch detection → segmentation → per-note pitch/time editing model → resynthesis → an editor surface), so it follows the repo's SFZ-spike precedent: a design doc in `docs/research/` with a GO/NO-GO that, on GO, seeds its own milestone. HAVE to build on, not duplicate: `take.autoAlign` (timing), clip `pitchShiftSemitones`, `ai.repaintAudio`, and m23-n transcription for word boundaries. **Scope split the spike MUST resolve explicitly, flagged now to avoid carrying ambiguity:** the user's "allow vocal import/plugins" covers two different things — importing vocal AUDIO files and hosting third-party pitch plugins (both fine, AU hosting already exists) versus importing third-party VOICE MODELS, which the own-voice-only policy forbids outright. State the line in the doc. Route: daw-architect. GATE: GO/NO-GO with a phase breakdown, a named resynthesis approach with a quality argument, and an honest estimate.

Order: a (shipped regression, first) → b → **c1 → c2** (m23-c SPLIT 2026-07-26 by the orch pass: c1 is the reproduced, self-contained off-clip fix; c2 is the two-surface auto-scroll feature whose piano-roll half may need new scroll infrastructure) → d → e → f → **g1 → g2 → g3** (m23-g SPLIT 2026-07-26 by the orch pass, along the gate's own two independent claims: g1 is atomicity proven on the edit with no geometry — selection + group DELETE as one undo step; g2 is the relative-offsets DRAG geometry and the non-contiguous-window data-loss hazard, with arrow-key nudge split OUT of it to x on 2026-07-26 as net-new and gate-absent; g3 is rubber band + track multi-select, LAST because shift/⌘ click alone already satisfies the whole gate and convenience must not gate correctness) → h (cheap-to-moderate editing ergonomics the user hits every session) → **k1 → k2 → k3 → k4a → k4b** (MIDI I/O, the biggest standalone capability gap; m23-k SPLIT 2026-07-26 by the orch pass into codec / inverse / project-mapping / surfaces, because the single line bundled four different kinds of risk — a high-risk binary parser, its inverse, a project-mapping decision needing a `daw-architect` read, and two wire triples plus a UI path. The split's load-bearing decision is that k1's IR is TICK-NATIVE, which is what keeps the tempo-map question inside k3 where the architect read is, instead of letting k1 answer it silently. **k4 was SPLIT AGAIN 2026-07-27 by the orch pass into k4a (export: domain + wire, ZERO UI) and k4b (the three UI surfaces), because the two halves have different routes, different gates, and different dependencies — k4b's drop target and Import menu need only k3, while its Export menu entry needs k4a. The split also forced the discovery that k4's original round-trip gate was VACUOUS: it passes under any beat→tick rounding rule on every fixture in the repo) → l → m (research then export delta) → n (packaging decided, see item) → **r → o** (the per-insert tap lands before the frequency-reference guidance that surfaces on top of it) → p → **s** (filed 2026-07-26 by the m23-a orch pass; pre-existing m22-e label truncation, now a codified-law violation — runs late because it is cosmetic and cheap, not because it is optional) → **t** (filed 2026-07-26 by the m23-c1 pass; the same honesty law on the piano-roll header's BAR readout — runs beside s because both are cheap relabels) → **u** (filed 2026-07-26 by the m23-d fix pass; the live-thru half of the render hole m23-d closed for audition — runs late because it is masked behind a cold-app/never-played/8-second-hold conjunction, NOT because it is cosmetic: it is a render-path correctness gap and needs a `daw-architect` read on m16-b3 §4.3 C11 before any code) → **v** (filed 2026-07-26 by the m23-e orch pass; the discoverability half of m23-e, deliberately split out — runs late because the mechanical path already works and this is polish on top of it) → **w** (filed 2026-07-26 by the m23-g1 orch pass; the wire+MCP half of group delete, consciously deferred to the zero-wire-growth preference and filed so the constitution's every-capability-ships-a-command rule is honored rather than quietly dropped) → **x** (filed 2026-07-26 by the m23-g2 orch pass; the arrow-key nudge that used to sit in g2's TITLE but exists nowhere in the code and nowhere in m23-g's gate — runs directly after g2 because it is a thin keyboard layer over the same group-move verb) → **y** (filed 2026-07-26 by the m23-g3 orch pass; track multi-select, split out of g3 because it had no gate leg there and its semantics question was open — answered on the line so it is the user's to overrule) → **z** (filed 2026-07-26 by the m23-h orch pass; the MIXER half of track reorder, split out because the mixer PARTITIONS tracks into channels+buses so its visual index needs a translation the arrange does not — an ungated index mapping riding along with the net-new ordering verb is the g1/g2/g3 pattern, and the computed discriminator is already written on the m23-h line for it to inherit) → q (q is a spike whose GO seeds M24). **Out of the run order by user decision 2026-07-26: `i` CLOSED no-action (the `" — Edited"` title marker already covers it), `j` DEFERRED (diagnosis filed, no action until the user asks).** Letters are additive in filing order, NOT run order — `r` was appended when the user reported the track-EQ spectrum gap, and the sequence above is authoritative. Deliberately NOT scheduled: nothing from this intake was dropped, but two asks resolved to existing surfaces rather than new work — out-of-range filtering (m22-a HP/LP already ships it; m23-o adds only the guidance) and note-editor playhead movement (mechanism verified live; m23-c covers the off-clip case the report most likely hit).

Not a revert.** The user responded to the (a)-(d) breakdown with architectural direction rather than a disposition, which settles keep-vs-revert implicitly: **(a) becomes PLUGGABLE** — the heavy model must NOT be linked into the app. Two plugin variants: one packaging the model + artifacts, one speaking **REST/OpenAPI to a cloud-hosted equivalent** (GCP/AWS, implemented later once the service is deployed). Goal in the user's words: *"package only essential app/components and leave the rest to the user to decide or implement."* **(b) `.gitignore` STANDS** — confirmed, large models never go in git. **(c) copy STANDS but into a separate PLUGIN FOLDER.** **(d) the resolver STANDS**, scoped to the plugin. **PLUGIN MECHANISM CHOSEN BY THE USER (asked explicitly 2026-07-27): RUNTIME-LOADABLE SWIFT BUNDLE** — the app ships with neither model, discovers plugins in a plugins folder, and dynamically loads them; third parties could write one. NOT build-time target selection, NOT the sidecar/REST-only shape. **THE SAME SEAM MUST SERVE M10's RVC WEIGHTS** — the user asked for identical treatment there (bundled-weights plugin + cloud-API plugin), so this is ONE design with two consumers, never two diverging designs. **FIVE PREMISES MEASURED 2026-07-27 before any design brief:** (1) the shipped app is **ad-hoc signed with NO hardened runtime** (`flags=0x2(adhoc)`, no `runtime` flag, no entitlements) so library validation does not bite TODAY — **but it will the moment pkg-b/c lands**, because notarization REQUIRES the hardened runtime and loading differently-signed plugin bundles then needs `com.apple.security.cs.disable-library-validation`. **This design and pkg-b/c/e are coupled; neither may be settled without the other.** (2) No `.entitlements` file exists in the repo. (3) Three `Sendable` provider-protocol precedents to model on: `CopilotProviding`, `SidecarManaging`, `VoiceConversionManaging`. (4) **NO target uses `-enable-library-evolution`** — plain SwiftPM — so a pure-Swift protocol across a `dlopen` boundary is ABI-FRAGILE and the design must pick its stable-ABI strategy deliberately (`@objc` + `NSBundle.principalClass`, a `@_cdecl` C entry point, or library evolution on a shared PluginAPI module). (5) **Full Xcode 26.6 IS present**, so building a loadable bundle is feasible — note SwiftPM alone emits libraries/executables, not loadable `.bundle`s. **CONSEQUENCE FOR THE LANDED WORK:** WhisperKit currently sits directly on **AIServices**, which contradicts the decision — it must move out of the main package graph into the plugin. Concrete target: the bundle went **27 MB → 36 MB** on 2026-07-27 because WhisperKit statically linked **6230 symbols**; the plugin design should hand that 9 MB back. **NEXT STEP: a `daw-architect` design pass covering both consumers — NOT YET DELEGATED (the loop is paused and the orchestrator does not spawn agents unbidden).** Original entry follows: **m23-n1 (and therefore all of m23-n) — waiting on a USER decision: the WhisperKit adoption was INTERRUPTED by the user 2026-07-27 at the moment of delegation.** Treating that as a decline rather than a hiccup, because the item's first act is a genuine one-way door and the interrupt landed exactly on it. Next cycle should NOT re-fire n1 without that word.
  **CORRECTION, same day, measured not assumed: THE n1 WORK ACTUALLY LANDED IN THE TREE.** The delegation returned an interrupt error to the orchestrator, which reported it as "did not go out" — **that report was wrong.** The agent had already run to completion. Present in the working tree right now: `Package.swift` carries the WhisperKit `exact: "0.18.0"` edge on **AIServices** (with a written version-rule rationale); `.gitignore` gained `/Models/` and `**/.cache/huggingface/`; **`Models/` holds the full 1.5 GB copy**; `Package.resolved` exists with 8 pins; `Sources/AIServices/WhisperModelCatalog.swift` (432 lines) and `Tests/AIServicesTests/WhisperModelCatalogTests.swift` (400 lines, 19 tests) are new. **Measured state: forced-full `--build-tests` exit 0, `[896/897]`, 42.90 s, ZERO warnings; `git check-ignore` confirms the weights are ignored and `git status -uall` stages ZERO of them.** So the tree is HEALTHY, not half-broken. **The item is still NOT ticked and must not be, because the user declined the action, and a clean build is not consent.** **REVERT IS THE DEFAULT, not one of two equal options.** The user declined this action; the action happened anyway; the tree now carries the declined state. - ~~M4 (i) live mid-play send activation~~ **RESOLVED 2026-07-06** (diagnostic round 3, per the parked method: stderr instrumentation + node-connection dump + preallocated-atomic tap probes + the live `diag-send.mjs` repro). Root cause was NOT the send wiring: the connection dump immediately after the rewire resume showed the mid-play-added leg fully attached and connected (source mixer → gain(vol 0.5) → bus sum → chain host → bus mixer, 48 kHz/2ch throughout) and the gain node's tap FIRING — carrying peak 0.0. Two stacked mechanisms produced the "silent until stop/play" symptom, on ALL strips (bus A metered 0.0000 after the resume too — the old E2E's poll windows just never caught it): (1) **THE BUG** — the resume anchored `derivedBeats` on `outputNode.lastRenderTime`, which right after the stop→reset→start bounce still reports the PREVIOUS render session's sample clock (valid flags set, measured sample=98304) while the restarted session's clock begins again near 0; the `max()` clamp then froze the playhead at the resume beat for the ENTIRE prior session length (unbounded in a long session — why it read as "silent forever"), postponing the loop-wrap revival past every poll (predicted wrap +4.19 s vs observed +4.17 s, 36 ms match). (2) The repro material (7.5-beat sustained MIDI chord) has no note-on between the resume beat and the wrap, and no-chase is the settled v0 contract (`MIDIEventSchedule`, ARCHITECTURE.md), so the resumed schedule was legitimately silent until the wrap — "stop/play revives it" was simply a restart from beat 0 with fresh note-ons, not a topology repair. FIX (one flag): the rewire resume calls `startPlayers(…, renderClockTrusted: false)`, forcing the already-proven host-clock anchor branch (host time is monotonic across the bounce; players start on hostTime anchors in both branches, so nothing else changes). Live post-fix: MIDI pad send meters on B at +2.29 s (= the wrap, level exactly matching the stop/play reference, no transport command, A uninterrupted-then-revived per the restart contract); audio-clip send meters at +0.26 s with A continuously intact; bus E2E 19/19 (fx-poll window widened to cover the no-chase wrap, with comment); suite 920/117 green; app stderr clean.
- ~~Teardown crash: `project.new` after a played send/bus project~~ **RESOLVED 2026-07-12 (m13-a) — the crash class is dead BY CONSTRUCTION: once-rendered engines are never surgically detached; teardown-class changes discard and rebuild the whole engine.** Full arc, kept as the institutional record: **(1) 2026-07-06, discovery** during M4 (i) verification — build track→busA output + send→busB, `transport.play` ~1 s, `stop`, `project.new`, next `track.add` → SIGSEGV inside `-[AVAudioEngine connect:to:format:]` → `AVAudioEngineGraph::UpdateGraphAfterReconfig` → `swift_unknownObjectRelease` on a garbage pointer (`DAWApp-2026-07-06-051956.ips`; a sibling run crashed in a SwiftUI executor check, `…-051559.ips` — two surface stacks = heap corruption); the never-played variant did not crash. **(2) 2026-07-10, M9 crash-a fix** — diagnosis "detach against a stopped engine is unsafe"; mechanism = retire bin (`pendingDetachNodes` parked while stopped, `flushRetiredNodes()` after restart); live-verified 3× that day. **(3) 2026-07-12, FALSIFICATION** — the M13 audit (audit-m13.md B1) hit 3/3 staging sessions SIGSEGV'ing inside the fix's OWN flush path, and the m13-a C0 instrumented experiment named the mechanism with node-level evidence: after ONE engine stop→reset→start boundary, the FIRST flush-detach — of the routed source's strip mixer, with the engine RUNNING and `isRunning=true` logged at the call — is itself the faulting frame (instrumented addr `0x9ba885060 AVAudioMixerNode`, no survive line, `DAWApp-2026-07-12-104059.ips`; independently reproduced via `track.remove`, `…-104254.ips`). So "running at detach time" was NEVER sufficient: a node that RENDERED and then sat attached across a stop→reset→start boundary is already stale inside `AVAudioEngineGraph`'s internal node vector, and any later detach of it faults. The audit's "m12-f regression" suspicion was WRONG — same crash on the pre-m12-f binary; M9's 3× verification was heap-reuse luck. The C0 control leg (fresh-launch `project.new` ×20, engine never ran) survived 20/20, formally retiring the audit's session-3 "crash on first wire command" anomaly as mis-attribution (that shape cannot reach the crashing frame; plausible source = a foreign wire client, the documented 2026-07-11 staging-port anomaly). **(4) Resolution (m13-a; design docs/research/design-m13a-teardown-crash.md, supersedes fix-teardown-crash.md)** — retire-bin machinery deleted; announce-class reconcile on a once-rendered engine ABORTS before the first topology mutation and `AudioEngine.rebuildEngine(reason:)` discards the whole AVAudioEngine + PlaybackGraph and cold-builds from the model; `projectWillReplace()` forces the same rebuild unconditionally at project boundaries; `teardownDetach` detaches directly only against a running or never-run engine. Proven by the m13-a campaign: audit recipes verbatim + 12/12 played/unplayed × routed/plain matrix + 3× 41-track perf-c + 50-cycle soak (100 rebuilds, +0.4 MB RSS) + 20× fresh-launch — zero crashes, zero new .ips, m12-b offline anchors byte-identical, worst 41-track rebuild 143 ms.

Residual, recorded honestly: no AUv3 exists on this machine, so the out-of-process AUv3 custom-view leg is untested by necessity (SKIPPED, never faked); the ladder degrades to the generic body + warning by design if one ever fails to serve a view.
- ~~Input device selection: pinned-device capture yields empty takes~~ **RESOLVED 2026-07-05.** Root cause was NOT the eager pin wedging the AUHAL (disproved by CLI repro: eager prepare + pin still delivers tap buffers). Two stacked bugs: (1) the same-device guard compared the pin target against the AUHAL's `CurrentDevice`, which on a fresh AVAudioEngine is the engine's PRIVATE AGGREGATE device — never equal to any raw hardware ID — so every pinned take (default uid included) performed a real device set; (2) that set makes AVFoundation post `AVAudioEngineConfigurationChange` on the capture engine ~65 ms after start with the engine still running and the tap still healthy, and the config-change handler treated it as fatal → `stopRecording()` finalized the writer before the first buffer, and the writer's post-finalize guard silently swallowed every later delivery (hence zero frames AND zero stderr). Fix: pin skips when the uid resolves to the current system default (`InputDevices.defaultInputDeviceID()`); config-change handler only ends the take when the capture engine actually stopped; two-phase `InputCapture.prepare()/start(writer:)` replaces the eager pin; 1.5 s first-buffer watchdog aborts loudly ("no audio arrived from input device …") instead of a silent empty take. Live E2E green: default-uid pin, ZoomAudioDevice pin (frames flowed), unpinned.
